\begin{array}{l} \eta \frac {1}{n} \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {a - 1} + x (\mathbf {w} _ {b - 1} - \mathbf {w} _ {a - 1})) d x\right) (\mathbf {w} _ {a - 1} - \mathbf {w} _ {b - 1}) \| \\ = \| \left(\mathbf {I} - \frac {\eta}{n} \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {a - 1} + x (\mathbf {w} _ {b - 1} - \mathbf {w} _ {a - 1})) d x\right)\right) (\mathbf {w} _ {a - 1} - \mathbf {w} _ {b - 1}) \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

The derivation above uses the update rule of gradient descent and Cauchy mean-value theorem. Then according to Cauchy Schwarz inequality and strong convexity, it can be further bounded as  $\| \mathbf{w}_a - \mathbf{w}_b\| \leq (1 - \eta \mu)\| \mathbf{w}_{a - 1} - \mathbf{w}_{b - 1}\|$ .

This can be used iteratively, which ends up with the following inequality:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} _ {a} - \mathbf {w} _ {b} \right\| \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {z} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {a - z} - \mathbf {w} _ {b - z} \right\| \tag {S27}
$$

which indicates that  $d_{j_k,j_q} \leq (1 - \eta \mu)^z d_{j_k - z,j_q - z}$  and thus  $d_{j_k,j_q} \leq d_{j_k - z,j_q - z}$ . So by replacing  $z$  with  $j_k$ , we will have:  $d_{j_k,j_q} \leq (1 - \mu \eta)^{j_k} d_{0,j_q - j_k}$ .

![](images/d0590948aa076e1a71056c695174d7eaa9ec36ef7ea2f264881ddcf24e3ca07d.jpg)

# A.2.4. MAIN RECURSIONS

We bound the difference between  $\mathbf{w}^I_t$  and  $\mathbf{w}^U_t$ . The proofs of the theorems stated below are in the following sections.

Our proof starts out with the usual approach of trying to show a contraction for the gradient updates, see e.g., (Bottou et al., 2018). First we bound  $\| \mathbf{w}_t - \mathbf{w}^U_t\|$ , i.e.:

Theorem 4 (Bound between iterates on full and the leave-  $r$ -out dataset).  $\| \pmb{w}_t - \pmb{w}^U_t\| \leq M_1\frac{r}{n}$  where  $M_{1} = \frac{2}{\mu} c_{2}$  is some positive constant that does not depend on  $t$ .

To show that the preliminary estimate on the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t\|$  used in Theorem 3 and Corollary 1 holds, the proof is provided as below:

Theorem 5 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones). Consider an iteration  $t$  indexed with  $j_{m}$  for which  $j_{m} \leq t < j_{m} + T_{0} - 1$ , and suppose that we are at the  $x$ -th iteration of full gradient updates, so  $j_{1} = j_{0} + xT_{0}$ ,  $j_{m} = j_{0} + (m - 1 + x)T_{0}$ . Suppose that we have the bounds  $\| H_{t} - B_{j_{m}} \| \leq \xi_{j_{1},j_{m}} = Ad_{j_{1},j_{m} + T_{0} - 1} + \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} AM_{1}\frac{r}{n}$  (where we recalled the definition of  $\xi$ ) and  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} \leq \frac{\mu}{2}$  for all iterations  $x$ . Then

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {t + 1} \right\| \leq \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}}} \leq \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

Recall that  $c_{0}$  is the Lipschitz constant of the Hessian,  $M_{1}$  and  $A$  are defined in Theorem 4 and Corollary 1 respectively, which do not depend on  $t$ ,

For this theorem, note that this inequality depends on the condition  $\| \mathbf{H}_t - \mathbf{B}_{j_m}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}$  while in Theorem 3, to prove  $\| \mathbf{H}_t - \mathbf{B}_{j_m}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}$ , we need to use the inequality in Theorem 5, i.e.  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_{t + 1} - \mathbf{w}_{t + 1}\| \leq \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_1\frac{r}{n}$ . In what follows, we will show that both inequalities hold for all the iterations  $t$  without relying on other conditions.

We can select hyper-parameters  $T_0, j_0$  such that

$$
A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} - m + 1} d _ {0, (m - 1) T _ {0}} + \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n} <   \min (\frac {\mu}{2}, (1 - \frac {r}{n}) \mu - \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} r (n - r)}{2 n ^ {2}}),
$$

e.g. when  $m = 2$  and  $T_0 = 5$ , which is what we used in our experiments. It is enough that

$$
j _ {0} > \max (\frac {\log (\frac {1}{A d _ {0 , 5}} [ \frac {\mu}{2} - \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) ]}{\log (1 - \eta \mu)}, \frac {\log (\frac {1}{A d _ {0 , 5}} [ (1 - \frac {r}{n}) \mu - \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) ]}{\log (1 - \eta \mu)}) + m - 1.
$$

This holds for small enough  $r / n$ :

$$
j _ {0} > \frac {\log (\frac {1}{A d _ {0 , 5}} [ \frac {\mu}{2} - \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) ]}{\log (1 - \eta \mu)} + m - 1
$$

Then the following two theorems hold.

Theorem 6 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones (all iterations)). For any  $j_m < t < j_m + T_0 - 1$ ,  $\| \pmb{w}^I_t - \pmb{w}_t \| \leq \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_1 \frac{r}{n}$  and  $\| \pmb{H}_t - \pmb{B}_{j_m} \| \leq \xi_{j_1, j_m}$ .

Then we have the following bound for  $\| \mathbf{w}^U t - \mathbf{w}^I t\|$ , which is our main result.

Theorem 7 (Convergence rate of DeltaGrad). For all iterations  $t$ , the result  $w^{I}_{t}$  of DeltaGrad, Algorithm 1, approximates the correct iteration values  $w^{U}_{t}$  at the rate

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} \right\| = o \left(\frac {r}{n}\right).
$$

So  $\| \pmb{w}^U_t - \pmb{w}^I_t\|$  is of a lower order than  $\frac{r}{n}$ .

This is proved in Section A.2.8.

# A.2.5. PROOF OF THEOREM 4

Proof. By subtracting the GD update from equation (1), we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} ^ {U} - \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \\ - \eta \left(\frac {1}{n - r} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right) - \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right)\right) - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) \tag {S28} \\ \end{array}
$$

in which the right-hand side can be rewritten as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \eta (\nabla F (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) - \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t})) \\ - \eta \left(\frac {1}{n - r} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} t\right) - \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} t\right)\right) - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} t\right)\right) \\ = \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \eta (\nabla F (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) - \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t})) \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left(\frac {r}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right) - \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right)\right) \\ = \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \eta (\nabla F (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) - \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t})) + \delta_ {t}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by applying Cauchy mean value theorem, the triangle inequality, Cauchy Schwarz inequality and Lemma 7 respectively, we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t + 1} \right\| \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \eta \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x\right) \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right) \right\| + \| \delta_ {t} \| \\ \leq \| \mathbf {I} - \eta \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x \| \| \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {2 c _ {2} r \eta}{n} \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by applying the triangle inequality over integrals and Lemma 2, the formula can be further bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x\right) \| \| \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {2 c _ {2} r \eta}{n} \\ \leq (1 - \eta \mu) \| \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {2 c _ {2} r \eta}{n} \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by applying this formula iteratively, we get:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t + 1} \right\| \leq \frac {1}{\eta \mu} \frac {2 c _ {2} r \eta}{n} = \frac {2 c _ {2}}{\mu} \frac {r}{n} := M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}
$$

# A.2.6. PROOF OF THEOREM 5

Proof. The updates for the iterations  $j_m \leq t \leq j_m + T_0 - 1$  follow the Quasi-Hessian update. We proceed in a similar way as before, by expanding the recursion as below:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} \right\| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} - \eta \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) \right. \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left(n \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \right] - \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t}\right)\right) \| \tag {S29} \\ = \| (\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}}) (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}) - \frac {r \eta}{n - r} \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t}) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

By rearranging the formula above and using the triangle inequality, we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \| \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) - \frac {r \eta}{n - r} \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) \| \\ \leq \left\| \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \right\| \tag {S30} \\ + \left\| \frac {r \eta}{n - r} \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right\| + \left\| \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right\| \\ \end{array}
$$

in which we use  $\mathbf{H}_{t,i}$  to denote  $\int_0^1\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}_t + x(\mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t))dx$  (recall that  $\mathbf{H}_i$  represents the Hessian matrix evaluated at the  $i_{th}$  sample). Then the terms in the first absolute value are rewritten as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} [ \mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n}{n - r} (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} + \mathbf {H} _ {t}) ] (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}) \\ = \left[ \mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) \right] \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

which uses the fact that  $\mathbf{H}_t = \sum_{i=1}^n \mathbf{H}_{t,i} = \sum_{i \notin R} \mathbf{H}_{t,i} + \sum_{i \in R} \mathbf{H}_{t,i}$ . Then Formula (S30) can be further bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \| \left[ \mathbf {I} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \right] \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \| + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \| \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \| \\ + \left\| \frac {r \eta}{n - r} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \right\| + \left\| \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \right\| \tag {S31} \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| + \frac {r \eta c _ {2}}{n - r} + \frac {\eta r c _ {2}}{n - r} \\ \end{array}
$$

Then according to Lemma 8,  $d_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1} = d_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (x + m)T_0 - 1}$  decreases with increasing  $x$ , and thus  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} = Ad_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1} + \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} AM_1\frac{r}{n}$  is also decreasing with increasing  $x$ . So the formula above can be further bounded as:

$$
\leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| + \frac {2 r \eta c _ {2}}{n - r}
$$

This shows a recurrent inequality for  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t\|$ . Next, notice that the conditions for deriving the above inequality hold for all  $j_m \leq t \leq j_m + T_0 - 1$ .

Then, when we reach  $t = j_{m}$ , we have an iteration where the gradient is computed exactly. For these iterations we have  $\mathbf{w}_{t+1}^{I} = \mathbf{w}_{t}^{I} - \frac{\eta}{n-r} \sum_{i \notin R} \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t}^{I})$  as well as  $\mathbf{w}_{t+1} = \mathbf{w}_{t} - \eta \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t})$ . Using the same argument as in the bound for  $\mathbf{w}_{t} - \mathbf{w}_{t}^{U}$  we can get:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} \right\| \leq \left[ 1 - \eta \mu \right] \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} \right\| + \frac {2 c _ {2} r \eta}{n}.
$$

Therefore, we effectively have  $\xi = 0$  for these iterations. We then continue with  $t\gets t - 1$ , and use the appropriate bound among the two derived above. This recursive process works until we reach  $t = 1$ .

As long as  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0}\leq \frac{\mu}{2}, - \eta \mu +\eta \frac{n}{n - r}\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0} <   - \eta \mu +\eta \frac{n}{n - r}\frac{\mu}{2} <  0$  . Then we get the following inequality:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \right\| \leq \frac {2 \frac {\eta r c _ {2}}{n - r}}{\eta \mu - \eta \frac {n}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}}} \\ = \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}}} \\ \end{array}
$$

As long as  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0}\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , then

$$
\| \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| \leq \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}}} \leq \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \frac {\mu}{2}} = \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

The last step uses the fact that  $M_1 = \frac{2c_2}{\mu}$ .

# A.2.7. PROOF OF THEOREM 6

Architecture of the proof. To visualize the recursive proof process, we draw a picture as:

![](images/ca9592e51c3c4e6b98be0801e85c4bbf33dd996ce852d1c7d671192c986d5312.jpg)

Proof. First of all, in terms of the bound on  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}$  which is required in Theorem 5, i.e.  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} \leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , we do the analysis below to show that we can adjust the value of  $j_0$  and  $T_0$  such that it can hold for all  $t$ . When  $j_1 \geq j_0$ , i.e.  $j_1 = j_0 + xT_0$ , then

$$
\left\{j _ {2}, j _ {3}, \dots , j _ {m} \right\} = \left\{j _ {0} + (x + 1) T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + 2) T _ {0}, \dots , j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0} \right\},
$$

thus  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} = \xi_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (x + m - 1)T_0} = Ad_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (x + m)T_0 - 1} + \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} AM_1\frac{r}{n}$ . Here  $d_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (x + m)T_0 - 1}$  decreases with  $x$ , and so does  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} = \xi_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (x + m - 1)T_0}$ . So the following inequality holds:

$$
d _ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + m) T _ {0} - 1} \leq d _ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + m T _ {0} - 1} \leq (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1}.
$$

When  $j_1 < j_0$ , there are only  $m$  different choices for  $\{j_1, j_2, \ldots, j_m\}$ , in which the smallest  $j_1$  used for approximation is  $j_0 - m + 1$ . Then, the following inequality holds:

$$
d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {1}} d _ {0, j _ {m} - j _ {1}} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} - m + 1} d _ {0, j _ {m} - j _ {1}}.
$$

For those  $j_1, j_2, \ldots, j_m$ , we have  $j_m - j_1 \leq (m - 1)T_0$  and thus

$$
d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {1}} d _ {0, j _ {m} - j _ {1}} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} - m + 1} d _ {0, j _ {m} - j _ {1}} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} - m + 1} d _ {0, (m - 1) T _ {0}}.
$$

So  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}$  is bounded by  $A(1 - \eta \mu)^{j_0 - m + 1}d_{0,(m - 1)T_0} + \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} AM_1\frac{r}{n}$ . To make sure  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} \leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , we can adjust  $j_0,m,T_0$  to make  $A(1 - \eta \mu)^{j_0 - m + 1}d_{0,(m - 1)T_0} + \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} AM_1\frac{r}{n}$  smaller than  $\frac{\mu}{2}$ .

Then when  $t \leq j_0$ , the gradient is evaluated explicitly, which means that  $\mathbf{w}^U_t = \mathbf{w}^I_t$ , so the bound clearly holds, i.e., from Theorem 4, we have  $\| \mathbf{w}^U_t - \mathbf{w}_t \| \leq \frac{M_1 r}{n}$  and thus  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t \| = \| \mathbf{w}^U_t - \mathbf{w}_t \| \leq \frac{M_1 r}{n} \leq \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_1 \frac{r}{n}$ .

When  $j_0 < t < j_0 + T_0$ , in order to compute  $\mathbf{w}^I_t$ , we need to use the history information  $\{\Delta w_{j_1}, \Delta w_{j_2}, \dots, \Delta w_{j_m}\}$ ,  $\{\Delta g_{j_1}, \Delta g_{j_2}, \dots, \Delta g_{j_m}\}$  and the corresponding quasi-Hessian matrices  $\{\mathbf{B}_{j_1}, \mathbf{B}_{j_2}, \dots, \mathbf{B}_{j_m}\}$  where  $\{j_1, j_2, \dots, j_m\} = \{j_0 - m + 1, j_0 - m + 2, \dots, j_0\}$  (we suppose  $m < j_0$ , which is a natural assumption). Since  $\|\mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t\| \leq \frac{M_1r}{n}$  for any  $t \leq j_0$ , the conditions of Corollary 1 (used here with the  $j_1, \dots, j_m$  described above) hold up to  $j_0$ , so when  $t = j_0 + 1$ ,  $\|\mathbf{H}_{t-1} - \mathbf{B}_{j_m}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}$  where

$$
\xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} = \xi_ {j _ {0} - m + 1, j _ {0}} = A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n} = A d _ {j _ {0} - m + 1, j _ {0} + T _ {0}} + A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

Plus, according to Theorem 5,  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t\| \leq \frac{2rc_2 / n}{(1 - r / n)\mu - \xi_{j_1,j_m}} = \frac{2rc_2 / n}{(1 - r / n)\mu - \xi_{j_0 - m + 1,j_0}}$ . When  $\xi_{j_0 - m + 1,j_0}\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , then

$$
\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| \leq \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \xi_ {j _ {1} , j _ {m}}} = \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \frac {\mu}{2}} = \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

So the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t\|$  holds for all  $t\leq j_0 + 1$ . Then according to the conditions of Corollary 1, when  $t = j_{0} + 2$ $\| \mathbf{H}_{t - 1} - \mathbf{B}_{j_m}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}$  holds. This can proceed recursively until  $t = j_0 + T_0$ , in which the gradients are explicitly evaluated according to Theorem 5, i.e.:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + T _ {0}} \right\| \leq \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \xi_ {j _ {1} , j _ {m}}} = \frac {2 r c _ {2} / n}{(1 - r / n) \mu - \frac {\mu}{2}} = \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

Next when  $j_0 + T_0 < t < j_0 + 2T_0$ ,  $j_m$  is updated as  $j_0 + T_0$  while  $\{j_1, j_2, \ldots, j_{m-1}\}$  is updated as  $\{j_0 - m + 2, j_0 - m + 3, \ldots, j_0\}$  and we know that  $\| \mathbf{w}^{I}_{j_k} - \mathbf{w}_{j_k} \| \leq \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_1 \frac{r}{n}$ . So based on Corollary 1, the following inequality holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {t} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} = \xi_ {j _ {0} - m + 2, j _ {0} + T _ {0}} = A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n} \\ = A d _ {j _ {0} - m + 2, j _ {0} + 2 T _ {0}} + A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n} \\ \end{array}
$$

This process can proceed recursively.

When  $j_0 + xT_0 < t < j_0 + (x + 1)T_0$ , we know that:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {H} _ {t} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} = A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

Then based on Theorem 5,  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t \| \leq \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_1 \frac{r}{n}$ . Then at iteration  $j_0 + (x + 1)T_0$ , we update  $j_1, j_2, \ldots, j_m$  as:  $j_m \gets j_0 + (x + 1)T_0$  and  $j_{i-1} \gets j_i$  ( $i = 2, 3, \ldots, m$ ) and thus

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {k}} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {k}} \right\| \leq \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}
$$

still holds for all  $k = 1,2,\ldots ,m$

So when  $j_0 + (x + 1)T_0 < t < j_0 + (x + 2)T_0$ , Corollary 1 and Theorem 5 are applied alternatively. Then the following two inequalities hold for all iterations  $t$  satisfying  $j_0 + (x + 1)T_0 < t < j_0 + (x + 2)T_0$ :

$$
\left\| \mathbf {H} _ {t} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} = A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + A M _ {1} \frac {r}{n},
$$

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {k}} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {k}} \right\| \leq \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}.
$$

So in the end, we know that:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \right\| \leq \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \mathbf {H} _ {t} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}}
$$

hold for all  $t$ .

![](images/d23a67b6f624e891b5f64ad4e65694cc89ce6a7a2da18e0fe1410294ddc7617f.jpg)

# A.2.8. PROOF OF THEOREM 7

Proof. The proof is by induction.

When  $t \leq j_0$ , the gradient is evaluated explicitly, which means that  $\mathbf{w}^U_t = \mathbf{w}^I_t$ , so the bound clearly holds.

From iteration  $T_{0}$  to iteration  $t$ , the difference between  $\mathbf{w}^I_t$  and  $\mathbf{w}^U_t$  can be bounded as follows. In these equations, we use the definition of the update formula  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{w}^{I}_t - \frac{\eta}{n - r} [n(\mathbf{B}_{j_m}(\mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t)) - \sum_{i\in R}\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t)]$ . By rearranging terms appropriately, we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t + 1} \right\| = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right] \right. \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t}\right) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right) \| \tag {S32} \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by bringing in  $\mathbf{H}_t$  into the expression above, it is rewritten as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \mathbf {H} _ {t} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right] \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \left(\nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t}\right) - \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right) \| \tag {S33} \\ \end{array}
$$

In the formula above, we will try to make sure there is no confusion between  $\mathbf{H}_t(\mathbf{w})$  (Hessian as a function evaluated at  $\mathbf{w}$ ) and  $\mathbf{H}_t \times (\mathbf{w})$  (Hessian times a vector). Then by applying the Cauchy mean value theorem over each individual  $\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t) - \nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}_t)$  and by denoting the corresponding Hessian matrix as  $\mathbf{H}_{t,i}$  (note that  $\sum_{i=1}^{n} \mathbf{H}_{t,i} = n\mathbf{H}_t$ ), the expression becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \mathbf {H} _ {t} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right] \right. \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \in R} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) + \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}\right) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the fact that  $\sum_{i\in R}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}_t) + \sum_{i\notin R}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}_t) = n\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)$  and  $\sum_{i\in R}\mathbf{H}_{t,i} + \sum_{i\notin R}\mathbf{H}_{t,i} = n\mathbf{H}_t$ , the expression can be rearranged as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \not \in R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}) - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}) (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right] \right. \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

in which  $\frac{\eta}{n - r}\sum_{i\in R}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}_t)$  is canceled out. Then by adding and subtracting  $\mathbf{w}^U_t$  in the first part, we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}) (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) \right] \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \right] \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

We apply Cauchy mean value theorem over  $-\frac{\eta}{n - r}\sum_{i\notin R}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}_t) + \frac{\eta}{n - r}\sum_{i\notin R}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^U_t)$ , i.e.:

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {t}) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \not \in R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) \\ = \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left[ \sum_ {i \not \in R} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x \right] \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

In addition, note that  $\mathbf{H}_{t,i} = \int_0^1\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}_t + x(\mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t))dx$ . So the formula above becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \| \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} \times (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U}) - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}) (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U}) \right] \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) - \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \right] \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \not \in R} \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x\right) \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by applying the triangle inequality and rearranging the expression appropriately, the expression can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \| (\mathbf {I} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i}) (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) \| + \| \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}) (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t}) \right] \| \\ + \| \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left[ \sum_ {i \notin R} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right)\right) d x \right] \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \| \\ + \left\| \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \left[ (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} - \mathbf {H} _ {t}) (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t}) \right] \right\|, \\ \end{array}
$$

in which the first term is the main contraction component which always appears in the analyses of gradient descent type algorithms. The remaining terms are error terms due to the various sources of error: using a quasi-Hessian, not having a quadratic objective (implicitly assumed by the local models at each step), using the iterate  $\mathbf{w}^I$  for our update instead of the correct  $\mathbf{w}^U$ .

Then by using the following facts:

1.  $\| \mathbf{I} - \eta \mathbf{H}_{t,i}\| \leq 1 - \eta \mu$  
2. from Theorem 6 on the approximation accuracy of the quasi-Hessian to mean Hessian, we have the error bound  $\| \mathbf{H}_t - \mathbf{B}_{j_m}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}$  
3. we can bound the difference of integrated Hessians using the strategy from equation (S20);  
4. from Theorem 4, we have the error bound  $\| \mathbf{w}^U_t - \mathbf{w}_t \| \leq M_1 \frac{r}{n}$  (and this requires no additional assumptions),

the expression above can be bounded as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {\eta c _ {0}}{2} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} \| \| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| \\ + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| \tag {S34} \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} r \eta}{2 n}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {M _ {1} r \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall from Corollary 1 that  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} = \xi_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (m + x - 1)T_0} = Ad_{j_0 + xT_0,j_0 + (m + x)T_0 - 1} + A\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_1\frac{r}{n}$  decreases with the increasing  $x$ . So the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} r \eta}{2 n}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {M _ {1} r \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}}. \tag {S35}
$$

Also by plugging the formula for  $\xi$  into the formula above and using Lemma 8 (contraction of GD updates), we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} r \eta}{2 n}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {1} r \eta}{n - r} \left(A d _ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m + x) T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}\right) \tag {S36} \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {n \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} r \eta}{2 n}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {1} r \eta}{n - r} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, we will argue that it is possible to choose hyperparameters such that  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0} \leq (1 - \frac{r}{n})\mu - \frac{c_0M_1r(n - r)}{2n^2}$ . Then  $1 - \eta \mu + \frac{n\eta}{n - r}\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0} + \frac{c_0M_1r\eta}{2n}$  is a constant for all  $t$  and smaller than 1. By denoting  $\mu - \frac{n}{n - r}\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0} - \frac{c_0M_1r}{2n}$  as  $C$ , the formula above can be written as:

$$
= (1 - \eta C) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| + \frac {M _ {1} r \eta}{n - r} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}).
$$

This can be used recursively until iteration  $j_{m} = j_{0} + (x + m)T_{0} - 1$ , i.e.:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq (1 - \eta C) ^ {t - (j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}) - 1} \| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} ^ {U} \| \\ + \frac {1 - (1 - \eta C) ^ {t - (j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0})}}{\eta C} \frac {M _ {1} r \eta}{n - r} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) \\ \leq (1 - \eta C) ^ {t - (j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}) - 1} \| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} ^ {U} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) \\ \end{array}
$$

We can set  $t = j_0 + (y + m)T_0$  and for any  $y = 1,2,\ldots ,x - 1$ , the formula above can be rewritten as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {U} \right\| \\ \leq (1 - \eta C) ^ {T _ {0} - 1} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} \left(A \left(1 - \eta \mu\right) ^ {j _ {0} + y T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Then at the iteration  $t = j_0 + (y + m - 1)T_0$ , the gradient is explicitly evaluated, which means that:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} \right\| \leq (1 - \eta \mu) \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0}} \right\|.
$$

Since  $C = \mu -\frac{n}{n - r}\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0} - \frac{c_0M_1r}{2n}$ , then  $1 - \eta \mu < 1 - \eta C$  and thus

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0} + 1} \right\| \leq (1 - \eta C) \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0}} \right\|,
$$

which can be plugged into the formula above:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {U} \right\| \\ \leq (1 - \eta C) ^ {T _ {0}} \| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {U} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} \left(A \left(1 - \eta \mu\right) ^ {j _ {0} + y T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

This can be used recursively over  $y = x - 1, x - 2, \ldots, 2, 1$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {U} \right\| \\ \leq (1 - \eta C) ^ {y T _ {0}} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} \| \\ + \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {(y - p) T _ {0}} \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + p T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}) \\ = (1 - \eta C) ^ {y T _ {0}} \| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} ^ {U} \| \tag {S37} \\ + \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {(y - p) T _ {0}} \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + p T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1}) \\ + \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {(y - p) T _ {0}} \frac {A M _ {1} ^ {2} r ^ {2}}{C (n - r) (n / 2 - r)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

in which

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {(y - p) T _ {0}} \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + p T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1}) \\ = \frac {A M _ {1} r \eta}{C (n - r)} (1 - \eta C) ^ {y T _ {0}} (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {- p T _ {0}} (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {p T _ {0}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall that since  $1 - \eta C > 1 - \eta \mu$ , then the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {(y - p) T _ {0}} \frac {M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} (A (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + p T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1}) \\ \leq \frac {A M _ {1} r \eta}{C (n - r)} (1 - \eta C) ^ {y T _ {0}} (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} \frac {1}{1 - (\frac {1 - \eta \mu}{1 - \eta C}) ^ {T _ {0}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Also  $\sum_{p=1}^{y}(1 - \eta C)^{(y-p)T_0}\frac{AM_1^2r^2}{C(n-r)(n/2-r)}$  can be simplified to:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p = 1} ^ {y} (1 - \eta C) ^ {(y - p) T _ {0}} \frac {A M _ {1} ^ {2} r ^ {2}}{C (n - r) (n / 2 - r)} = \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {y - 1} (1 - \eta C) ^ {p T _ {0}} \frac {A M _ {1} ^ {2} r ^ {2}}{C (n - r) (n / 2 - r)} \\ \leq \frac {1}{1 - (1 - \eta C) ^ {T _ {0}}} \frac {A M _ {1} ^ {2} r ^ {2}}{C (n - r) (n / 2 - r)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

So equation (S37) can be further bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} \right\| \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta C\right) ^ {y T _ {0}} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} \right\| \\ + \frac {A M _ {1} r}{C (n - r)} \left(1 - \eta C\right) ^ {y T _ {0}} \left(1 - \eta \mu\right) ^ {j _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} \frac {1}{1 - \left(\frac {1 - \eta \mu}{1 - \eta C}\right) ^ {T _ {0}}} \tag {S38} \\ + \frac {1}{1 - (1 - \eta C) ^ {T _ {0}}} \frac {A M _ {1} ^ {2} r ^ {2}}{C (n - r) (n / 2 - r)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

When  $t\to \infty$  and thus  $y\rightarrow \infty$ $(1 - \eta C)^{yT_0}\rightarrow 0$  and thus

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {U} \right\| = o (\frac {r}{n}).
$$

# A.3. Results for stochastic gradient descent

# A.3.1. QUASI-NEWTON

We modify Equations (S13) and (S12) to SGD versions:

$$
\mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {k + 1}} = \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} - \frac {\mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \Delta w ^ {S _ {j _ {k}} ^ {T}} \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}}}{\Delta w ^ {S _ {j _ {k}} ^ {T}} \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}}} + \frac {\Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} ^ {T}}{\Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} ^ {T} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}}} \tag {S39}
$$

$$
\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {k + 1}} ^ {S - 1} = \left(\mathbf {I} - \frac {\Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S} \Delta g _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T}}{\Delta g _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T} \Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S}}\right) \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S - 1} \left(\mathbf {I} - \frac {\Delta g _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S} \Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T}}{\Delta g _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T} \Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S}}\right) + \frac {\Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S} \Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T}}{\Delta g _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T} \Delta w _ {j _ {k}} ^ {S T}} \tag {S40}
$$

This iteration has the same initialization as  $\mathbf{B}_{j_k}$  and  $\mathbf{B}_{j_k}^{-1}$  but relies on the history information collected from the SGD-based training process  $[\Delta w^{S}_{j_0},\Delta w^{S}_{j_1},\dots,\Delta w^{S}_{j_{m - 1}}]$  and  $[\Delta g^{S}_{j_0},\Delta g^{S}_{j_1},\dots,\Delta g^{S}_{j_{m - 1}}]$  where  $\Delta w^{S}_{j_x} = \mathbf{w}^{S}_{j_x} - \mathbf{w}^{I,S}_{j_x}$  and  $\Delta g^{S}_{j_x} = G_{B,S}(\mathbf{w}^{I,S}_{j_x}) - G_{B,S}(\mathbf{w}^{S}_{j_x})$  ( $x = 0,1,2,\dots,m - 1$ ). By the same argument as the proof of Lemma 6, the following inequality holds:

$$
K _ {1} \| \mathbf {z} \| ^ {2} \leq \mathbf {z} ^ {T} \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \mathbf {z} \leq K _ {2} \| \mathbf {z} \| ^ {2} \tag {S41}
$$

where  $K_{1} \coloneqq \frac{1}{(1 + \frac{L}{\mu})^{2m}\frac{L}{\mu} + \frac{1 - (1 + \frac{L}{\mu})^{2m}}{1 - (1 + \frac{L}{\mu})^{2}}\frac{1}{\mu}}$  and  $K_{2} \coloneqq (m + 1)L$ , which are both positive values representing a lower bound and an upper bound on the eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{B}^{S}_{jk}$ .

# A.3.2. PROOF PRELIMINARIES

Similar to the argument for the GD-version of DeltaGrad, we can give an upper bound on  $\delta_{t,S}$ :

Lemma 9 (Upper bound on  $\delta_{t,S}$ ). Define  $\delta_{t,S} = G_{B,S}(\pmb{w}^{U,S}_t) - G_{B - \Delta B,S}^U (\pmb{w}^{U,S}_t)$ . Then  $\| \delta_{t,S}\| \leq 2c_2\frac{\Delta B_t}{B}$ . Moreover, with probability higher than  $1 - t\times 2\exp (-2\sqrt{B})$

$$
\| \delta_ {t ^ {\prime}, S} \| \leq 2 c _ {2} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}})
$$

uniformly over all iterations  $t^{\prime}\leq t$

Proof. Recall that

$$
G _ {B, S} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}) = \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t})
$$

and

$$
G _ {B - \Delta B, S} ^ {U} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}) = \frac {1}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}, i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}).
$$

By subtracting  $G_{B,S}(\mathbf{w}^{U,S}_{t})$  from  $G_{B - \Delta B,S}^{U}(\mathbf{w}^{U,S}_{t})$ , we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| G _ {B - \Delta B, S} ^ {U} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}) - G _ {B, S} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}) \right\| \\ = \| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right) - \frac {1}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}, i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right) \| \\ = \| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}, i \in R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}) + \left(\frac {1}{B} - \frac {1}{B - \Delta B _ {t}}\right) \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}, i \notin R} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the triangle inequality and the fact that  $\| \nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^{U,S_t})\| \leq c_2$  (Assumption 3), the formula above can be bounded by  $\frac{2\Delta B_t c_2}{B}$ .

Because of the randomness from SGD, the  $r$  removed samples can be viewed as uniformly distributed among all  $n$  training samples. Each sample is included in a mini-batch according to the outcome of a Bernoulli  $(r / n)$  random variable  $\mathbf{S}_i$ . Within a single mini-batch  $\mathcal{B}_{t'}$  at the iteration  $t'$ , we get  $\mathbb{E}(\sum_{i\in \mathcal{B}_{t'}}\mathbf{S}_i) = \mathbb{E}(\Delta B_{t'}) = B\frac{r}{n}$  and  $\mathrm{Var}(\sum_{i\in \mathcal{B}_{t'}}\mathbf{S}_i) = B\frac{r}{n} (1 - \frac{r}{n})$ . So in terms of the random variable  $\frac{\Delta B_{t'}}{B}$ , its expectation and variance will be  $\mathbb{E}(\frac{\Delta B_{t'}}{B}) = \frac{r}{n}$  and  $\mathrm{Var}(\frac{\Delta B_{t'}}{B}) = \frac{r}{Bn} (1 - \frac{r}{n})$ .

Then based on Hoeffding's inequality, the following inequality holds:

$$
\operatorname * {P r} (| \frac {\Delta B _ {t ^ {\prime}}}{B} - \frac {r}{n} | \leq \epsilon) \geq 1 - 2 \exp (- 2 \epsilon^ {2} B).
$$

Then by setting  $\epsilon = \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}$  the formula above can be written as:

$$
\Pr \left(\left| \frac {\Delta B _ {t ^ {\prime}}}{B} - \frac {r}{n} \right| \geq \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \leq 2 \exp (- 2 \sqrt {B})
$$

Then by taking the union for all the iterations before  $t$ , we get: with probability higher than  $1 - t \times 2 \exp(-2\sqrt{B})$ ,

$$
| \frac {\Delta B _ {t ^ {\prime}}}{B} - \frac {r}{n} | \leq \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}
$$

and thus

$$
\frac {\Delta B _ {t ^ {\prime}}}{B} \leq \frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} \tag {S42}
$$

for all  $t^{\prime}\leq t$

In what follows, we use  $\Psi_{1}$  to represent  $\Psi_{1} := 2\exp(-2\sqrt{B})$ , which goes to 0 with large  $B$ .

Next we provide a bound for the sum of random sampled Hessian matrices within a minibatch in SGD.

Theorem 8 (Hessian matrix bound in SGD). With probability higher than

$$
1 - 2 p \exp \left(- \frac {\log (2 p) \sqrt {B}}{4 + \frac {2}{3} \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}}\right),
$$

for a given iteration  $t$ ,  $\| \left(\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i\in \mathcal{B}_t}\pmb {H}_i(\pmb{w}^S_t)\right) - \pmb {H}(\pmb{w}^S_t)\| \leq L\left(\frac{\log^2(2p)}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}$  where  $p$  represents the number of model parameters.

Proof. We consider using the matrix Bernstein inequality, Lemma 5. We define the random matrix  $\mathbf{S}_i = \frac{\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}) - \mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w})}{B}$ $(i\in \mathcal{B}_t)$ . Due to the randomness from SGD, we know that  $\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}_i) = \mathbb{E}(\frac{\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}) - \mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w})}{B}) = \mathbf{0}$ . Using the sum  $\mathbf{Z}$  as required in Lemma 5,  $\mathbf{Z} = \left(\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i\in \mathcal{B}_t}\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w})\right) - \mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w})$ . Also note that  $\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w})$  and  $\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w})$  are both  $p\times p$  matrices, so  $d_1 = d_2 = p$  in Lemma 5.

Furthermore, for each  $\mathbf{S}_i = \frac{\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}) - \mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w})}{B}$ , its norm is bounded by  $\frac{2L}{B}$  based on the smoothness condition, which means that  $J = \frac{2L}{B}$  in Lemma 5. Then we can explicitly calculate the upper bound on  $E(\mathbf{S}_i\mathbf{S}_i^*)$  and  $V(\mathbf{Z})$ :

$$
\| \mathbb {E} (\mathbf {S} _ {i} \mathbf {S} _ {i} ^ {*}) \| \leq \mathbb {E} (\| \mathbf {S} _ {i} \mathbf {S} _ {i} ^ {*} \|) \leq \mathbb {E} (\| \mathbf {S} _ {i} \| \| \mathbf {S} _ {i} ^ {*} \|) \leq J ^ {2} = \frac {4 L ^ {2}}{B ^ {2}},
$$

$$
V (Z) \leq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \frac {4 L ^ {2}}{B ^ {2}} = \frac {4 L ^ {2}}{B}.
$$

Thus by plugging the above expression into equation (S9) and (S10), we get:

$$
P (\| \mathbf {Z} \| \geq x) = \Pr (\left\| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w}) \right\vert - \mathbf {H} (\mathbf {w}) \| \geq x) \tag {S43}
$$

$$
\leq (d _ {1} + d _ {2}) \exp \left(\frac {- x ^ {2}}{\frac {4 L ^ {2}}{B} + \frac {2 L x}{3 B}}\right) = 2 p \exp \left(\frac {- x ^ {2}}{\frac {4 L ^ {2}}{B} + \frac {2 L x}{3 B}}\right), \forall x \geq 0
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} (\| \mathbf {Z} \|) = \mathbb {E} \left(\left\| \left(\frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w})\right) - \mathbf {H} (\mathbf {w}) \right\|\right) \tag {S44} \\ \leq \sqrt {\frac {8 L ^ {2}}{B} \log (d _ {1} + d _ {2})} + \frac {2 L}{3 B} \log (d _ {1} + d _ {2}) = \sqrt {\frac {8 L ^ {2}}{B} \log (2 p)} + \frac {2 L}{3 B} \log (2 p). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by setting  $x = L\left(\frac{\log^2(2p)}{B}\right)^{1/4}$ , Equation (S43) becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr (\| \mathbf {Z} \| \geq L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}) \\ = \Pr \left(\| \left(\frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w})\right) - \mathbf {H} (\mathbf {w}) \| \geq L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}\right) \tag {S45} \\ \leq (2 p) \exp \left(\frac {- \frac {L ^ {2} \log (2 p)}{\sqrt {B}}}{\frac {4 L ^ {2}}{B} + \frac {2 L ^ {2}}{3 B} \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}}\right) = (2 p) \exp \left(- \frac {\log (2 p) \sqrt {B}}{4 + \frac {2}{3} \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

For large mini-batch size  $B$ , both  $L\left(\frac{\log^2(2p)}{B}\right)^{1/4}$  and  $(2p)\exp\left(-\frac{\log(2p)\sqrt{B}}{4 + \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{\log^2(2p)}{B}\right)^{1/4}}\right)$  are approaching 0.

In what follows, we use  $\Psi_{2}$  to denote the probability  $\Psi_{2} := (2p)\exp\left(-\frac{\log(2p)\sqrt{B}}{4 + \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{\log^{2}(2p)}{B}\right)^{1/4}}\right)$ .

Based on this result, we can derive an SGD version of Theorem 3 as below, which also relies on a preliminary estimate on the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^{I,S_t} - \mathbf{w}^{S_t}\|$ :

Theorem 9 (Error in mean Hessian, and in secant equation with incorrect quasi-Hessian for SGD). Suppose that  $\| w^{S}t' - w^{I,S}t'\| \leq M_1\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n} - \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}} \left( \frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}} \right)$  and

$$
\left\| \left(\frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t ^ {\prime}}} \boldsymbol {H} _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}}\right)\right) - \boldsymbol {H} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}}\right) \right\| \leq L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}
$$

hold for any  $t' \leq t$  where  $M_1 = \frac{2c_2}{\mu}$ ,  $\mu$  is from Assumption 2 and  $c_2$  is from Assumption 3. Let  $e = \frac{L(L + 1) + K_2L}{\mu K_1}$  for the upper and lower bounds  $K_1, K_2$  on the eigenvalues of the quasi-Hessian from equation (S41) and for the Lipshitz constant  $c_0$  of the Hessian. For any  $t_1, t_2$  such that  $1 \leq t_1 < t_2 \leq t$ , we have:

$$
\| \pmb {H} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S} - \pmb {H} _ {t _ {2}} ^ {S} \| \leq 2 L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + c _ {0} d _ {t _ {1}, t _ {2}} + 3 c _ {0} M _ {1} \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}).
$$

For any  $j_1, j_2, \ldots, j_m$  such that  $j_m \leq t' \leq j_m + T_0 - 1$  and  $t' \leq t$ , we have:

$$
\left\| \Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} - \boldsymbol {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \right\|
$$

$$
\leq \left[ (1 + e) ^ {j _ {q} - j _ {k} - 1} - 1 \right] \cdot \left[ 2 L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + c _ {0} d _ {j _ {k}, j _ {q}} + \frac {3 c _ {0} M _ {1}}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}) \right] \cdot s _ {j _ {m}, j _ {1}}.
$$

Here  $s_{j_m,j_1} = \max \left(\| \Delta w^S_a\|\right)_{a = j_1,j_2,\ldots ,j_m}, d_{j_k,j_q} = \max \left(\| \pmb{w}^S_a - \pmb{w}^S_b\|\right)_{j_k\leq a\leq b\leq j_q}, \pmb{H}^{S}_{t}$  is the average of the Hessian matrix evaluated between  $\pmb{w}^{S}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{w}^{I,S}_{t}$  for the samples in mini-batch  $\mathcal{B}_t$ :

$$
\boldsymbol {H} ^ {S} _ {t} = \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \boldsymbol {H} _ {i} (\boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t} + x (\boldsymbol {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t})) d x.
$$

Proof. First of all, let us bound  $\| \mathbf{H}^{S}_{t_{1}} - \int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w}^{S}_{t_{1}} + x(\mathbf{w}^{I,S}_{t_{1}} - \mathbf{w}^{S}_{t_{1}}))dx\|$  by adding and subtracting  $\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i\in \mathcal{B}_{t_1}}\mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}^S_{t_1})$  and  $\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w}^{S}_{t_1})$  inside the norm:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S} - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} t _ {1} - \mathbf {w} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S}\right)\right) d x \right\| \\ = \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) d x - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) d x \| \\ = \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} (\mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x (\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}})) - \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}})) d x + \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}) \\ - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left(\mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) d x - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the triangle inequality and Assumption 4, the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \| \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) - \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \| d x \\ + \int_ {0} ^ {1} \| \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \| d x \\ + \left\| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \right\| \tag {S46} \\ \leq \frac {1}{B} \left(\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} c _ {0} x \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} \| d x\right) + \int_ {0} ^ {1} c _ {0} x \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} \| d x \\ + \| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \| \\ \leq c _ {0} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} \right\| + \left\| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \right\|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then based on the above results, we can compute the bound on  $\| \mathbf{H}^{S}_{t_1} - \mathbf{H}^{S}_{t_2}\|$ , for which we use the triangle inequality first:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S} - \mathbf {H} _ {t _ {2}} ^ {S} \right\| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {H} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) d x \right\| \\ + \left\| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) d x - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {2}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}}\right)\right) d x \right\| \tag {S47} \\ + \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {2}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}}\right)\right) d x - \mathbf {H} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the result from Formula (S46), this term can be further bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq c _ {0} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} \right\| + \left\| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {1}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \right\| \\ + c _ {0} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {2}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} \right\| + \left\| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t _ {2}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}}\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}}\right) \right\| \\ + \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t _ {1}} ^ {S} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}}\right)\right) d x - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t _ {2}} ^ {S} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {2}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}}\right)\right) d x \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since

$$
\left\| \left(\frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t ^ {\prime}}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}}\right)\right) - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}}\right) \right\| \leq L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}
$$

for any  $t' \leq t$ , then the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq 2 L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + c _ {0} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} \right\| + \frac {c _ {0}}{2} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} \right\| \\ + \frac {c _ {0}}{2} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {2}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} \| + c _ {0} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {1}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {1}} \| + c _ {0} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t _ {2}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t _ {2}} \| \\ = 2 L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + 3 c _ {0} M _ {1} \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) + c _ {0} d _ {t _ {1}, t _ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

This finishes the proof of the first inequality. Then by defining

$$
f = \left(2 L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + 3 c _ {0} M _ {1} \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) + c _ {0} d _ {j _ {k}, j _ {q}}\right) s _ {j _ {m}, j _ {1}}
$$

and using the same argument as Equation (S21)-(S23) (except that  $\Delta w$  and  $\Delta g$  are replaced with  $\Delta w^S$  and  $\Delta g^S$ ), the following inequality thus holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} b _ {j _ {q}} = \left\| \Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} - \left(\mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} - \frac {\mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta w ^ {S T} _ {j _ {q}} \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}}}{\Delta w ^ {S T} _ {j _ {q}} \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}}} + \frac {\Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta g ^ {S T} _ {j _ {q}}}{\Delta g ^ {S T} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}}}\right) \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \right\| \tag {S48} \\ \leq [ (1 + e) ^ {j _ {q} - j _ {k}} - 1 ] f \\ \end{array}
$$

and thus

$$
\left\| \Delta g ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} - \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {q}} \Delta w ^ {S} _ {j _ {k}} \right\| \leq \left[ (1 + e) ^ {j _ {q} - j _ {k} - 1} - 1 \right] f,
$$

which finishes the proof.

For simplicity, we denote  $M_1^S \coloneqq M_1 \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n} - \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}}$ . So the preliminary estimate of the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^{S}_{t'} - \mathbf{w}^{I,S}_{t'} \|$  becomes:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} \right\| \leq M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)
$$

![](images/609f6d5368fa3a5749ed2171380a198717841f26cd70ae207b0f45347d5fc368.jpg)

Similarly, we get a SGD-version of Corollary 1:

Corollary 2 (Approximation accuracy of Quasi-Hessian to mean Hessian). Suppose that  $\| \pmb{w}^{S}t^{\prime} - \pmb{w}^{I,S}t^{\prime}\| \leq M_1^S\left(\frac{r}{n} +\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}}\right)$  and

$$
\left\| \left(\frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t ^ {\prime}}} \boldsymbol {H} _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}}\right)\right) - \boldsymbol {H} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}}\right) \right\| \leq L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}
$$

hold for any  $t' \leq t$ .  $M_1$  and  $M_1^S$  are provided in Theorem 9, i.e.  $M_1 = \frac{2c_2}{\mu}$  and  $M_1^S = M_1\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n} - \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}}$ . Then for any  $t'$  and  $j_m$  such that  $j_m \leq t' \leq j_m + T_0 - 1$  and  $t' \leq t$ , the following inequality holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {H} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} - \boldsymbol {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} \\ := A \left(d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + 3 M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) + \frac {2}{c _ {0}} L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where recall again that  $c_{0}$  is the Lipschitz constant of the Hessian,  $d_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1}$  is the maximal gap between the iterates of the SGD algorithm on the full data from  $j_{1}$  to  $j_{m} + T_{0} - 1$  and  $A = \frac{c_0\sqrt{m[(1 + e)^m - 1]}}{c_1} + c_0$  in which  $e$  is a problem dependent constant defined in Theorem 9,  $c_{1}$  is the "strong independence" constant from Assumption (5).

This proof is similar to the proof of Corollary 1. First of all,  $\mathbf{H},\mathbf{B},\xi_{j_1,j_m}$  in Corollary 1 are replaced with  $\mathbf{H}^S,\mathbf{B}^S,\xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$ . Second, Theorem 9 holds and thus the following inequality holds:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {H} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} - \mathbf {H} ^ {S} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq 2 L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} + c _ {0} d _ {t ^ {\prime}, j _ {m}} + 3 c _ {0} M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)
$$

by using strong independence from Assumption 5,  $\| \mathbf{H}^{S}_{j_m} - \mathbf{B}^{S}_{j_m}\|$  can be bounded as:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {H} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {S} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {S} \right\| \leq \sqrt {m} \left[ (1 + e) ^ {m} - 1 \right] \frac {c _ {0}}{c _ {1}} \cdot \left(d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + 3 M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) + \frac {2}{c _ {0}} L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}\right) \tag {S49}
$$

Then by combining the two formulas above, we know that Corollary 2 holds. Note that the definition of  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$  can be rewritten as below:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} = A \left(d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + 3 M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) + \frac {2}{c _ {0}} L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}\right) \tag {S50} \\ =: A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} + A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} \\ \end{array}
$$

in which  $A_1 \coloneqq 3AM_1^S$  and  $A_2 \coloneqq 3AM_1^S + \frac{2AL(log(2p))^{1/2}}{c_0}$ .

We can do a similar analysis to Lemma 8 by simply replacing  $\mathbf{w}_t$  and  $F(*)$  with  $\mathbf{w}^{S_t}$  and  $G_{B,S}$ :

Lemma 10. Let us use the definition of  $d_{k,q}$  from Theorem 9:

$$
d _ {k, q} = \max  \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {a} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {b} \|\right) _ {k \leq a \leq b \leq q}
$$

where  $k < q \leq t$ , then  $d_{k,q} \leq (1 - \eta \mu)^k d_{0,q-j} + 2c_2\left(\frac{(\log(p+1))^2}{B}\right)^{1/4}$  holds with probability higher than  $1 - t(p+1) \exp\left(-\frac{\log(p+1)\sqrt{B}}{4 + \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{(\log(p+1))^2}{B}\right)^{1/4}}\right)$ .

Proof. According to Lemma 5, we can define a random matrix  $\mathbf{S}_i = \frac{1}{B} (\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^{S_a}) - \nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{S_a})$  where recall that  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{S_a}) = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\nabla F_{i,a}(\mathbf{w}^{S_a})(i\in \mathcal{B}_t)$ . Due to the randomness from SGD, we know that  $\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}_i) = \mathbf{0}$ . Based on the definition of  $\mathbf{Z}$  in Lemma 5,  $\mathbf{Z} = \frac{1}{B}\sum_{i\in \mathcal{B}_t}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^{S_a}) - \nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{S_a})$ . Also note that  $\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^{S_a})$  and  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{S_a})$  are both  $p\times 1$  matrices, so  $d_1 = p$  and  $d_2 = 1$  in Lemma 5.

Moreover according to Assumption 3,  $\| \nabla F_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{S}_{a})\| \leq c_{2}$ . Then we know that  $V(\mathbf{Z})\leq \frac{4c_2^2}{B}$  and  $\| \mathbf{S}_i\| \leq \frac{2c_2}{B}$ . So according to Lemma 5, the following inequality holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} P (\| \mathbf {Z} \| \geq x) = \Pr (\| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a}) - \nabla F (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a}) \| \geq x) \\ \leq \left(d _ {1} + d _ {2}\right) \exp \left(\frac {- x ^ {2}}{\frac {4 c _ {2} ^ {2}}{B} + \frac {2 c _ {2} x}{3 B}}\right) = (p + 1) \exp \left(\frac {- x ^ {2}}{\frac {4 c _ {2} ^ {2}}{B} + \frac {2 c _ {2} x}{3 B}}\right), \forall x \geq 0 \tag {S51} \\ \end{array}
$$

By setting  $x = c_{2}\left(\frac{(\log(p + 1))^{2}}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}$ , the formula above is evaluated as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr \left(\| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a}\right) - \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a}\right) \| \geq c _ {2} \left(\frac {\left(\log (p + 1)\right) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}\right) \\ \leq (p + 1) \exp \left(- \frac {\log (p + 1) \sqrt {B}}{4 + \frac {2}{3} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

So by taking the union for the first  $t$  iterations, then with probability higher than  $1 - t(p + 1)\exp \left(-\frac{\log(p + 1)\sqrt{B}}{4 + \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{(\log(p + 1))^2}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}}\right)$ , the following inequality holds for all  $t^\prime \leq t$ :

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t ^ {\prime}}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a}\right) - \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a}\right) \right\| \leq c _ {2} \left(\frac {\left(\log (p + 1)\right) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \tag {S52}
$$

Then by using the similar arguments to Lemma 8, we get:

$$
\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {b} \| \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {z} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a - z} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {b - z} \| + \frac {2 c _ {2}}{\mu} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} = (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {z} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {a - z} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {b - z} \| + M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}
$$

and thus  $d_{k,q} \leq (1 - \eta \mu)^k d_{0,q-k} + M_1\left(\frac{(\log(p+1))^2}{B}\right)^{1/4}$  holds with probability higher than  $1 - t(p + 1)\exp\left(-\frac{\log(p+1)\sqrt{B}}{4 + \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{(\log(p+1))^2}{B}\right)^{1/4}}\right)$ . In what follows, we use  $\Psi_3$  to denote  $(p+1)\exp\left(-\frac{\log(p+1)\sqrt{B}}{4 + \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{(\log(p+1))^2}{B}\right)^{1/4}}\right)$ .

Then by using the definition of  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$ , the following inequality holds with probability higher than  $1 - t\Psi_3$  for any  $x$  such that for  $j_0 + (x + m - 1)T_0\leq t$ , the following inequality holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} = \xi_ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {x T _ {0}} A d _ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + m T _ {0} - 1} \\ + A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \tag {S53} \\ \end{array}
$$

# A.3.3. MAIN RECURSIONS

We bound the difference between  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S_t}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}$ . First we bound  $\| \mathbf{w}^S t - \mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}\|$ :

Theorem 10 (Bound between iterates on full and the leave- $r$ -out dataset). When

$$
\frac {\Delta B _ {t ^ {\prime}}}{B} \leq \frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}
$$

holds for all  $t' < t$ ,  $\| \pmb{w}^S_t - \pmb{w}^{U,S}_t \| \leq \frac{2c_2}{\mu} \left( \frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}} \right)$ . Since with probability higher than  $1 - t \times \Psi_1$ ,

$$
\frac {\Delta B _ {t ^ {\prime}}}{B} \leq \frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}
$$

holds for all  $t' < t$ . Then with the same probability,  $\| \pmb{w}^{S}_{t' + 1} - \pmb{w}^{U,S}_{t' + 1}\| \leq M_1\left(\frac{r}{n} +\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}}\right)$  for all iterations  $t^{\prime} < t$ , where recall that  $M_{1} = \frac{2c_{2}}{\mu}$ .

Similarly, we can bound the difference between  $\mathbf{w}^I_t$  and  $\mathbf{w}_t$ .

Theorem 11 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones). Suppose that for some iteration  $t$  and any given  $t' \leq t$  such that  $j_m' \leq t' \leq j_m' + T_0 - 1$ , we have the following bounds:

1.  $\| H^{S}_{t^{\prime}} - B^{S}_{j_{m}^{\prime}}\| \leq \xi_{j_{1}^{\prime},j_{m}^{\prime}}^{S} = Ad_{j_{1}^{\prime},j_{m}^{\prime} + T_{0} - 1} + A\frac{3}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n}} M_{1}\left(\frac{r}{n} +\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}}\right) + A\frac{2}{c_{0}} L\left(\frac{\log^{2}(2p)}{B}\right)^{1 / 4};$  
2.  $\frac{\Delta B_{t'}}{B} \leq \frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}$ ;  
3. Formula (S53) holds for any  $x$  such that  $j_0 + (x + m - 1)T_0 \leq t$ ;  
4.  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0}^S +A\times M_1\left(\frac{(\log(p + 1))^2}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}\leq \frac{\mu}{2},$

then

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} \right\| \leq \frac {2 c _ {2}}{\left(\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \mu} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) = M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)
$$

for any  $t' \leq t$  Recall that  $c_0$  is the Lipshitz constant of the Hessian,  $M_1$  and  $A$  are defined in Theorem 10 and Corollary 2 respectively, and do not depend on  $t$ .

in particular for all  $t$ , the following inequality holds:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {S} _ {t + 1} \right\| \leq \frac {2 c _ {2}}{\left(\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \mu} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) = M _ {1} ^ {S} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right).
$$

Similarly, we will show that both inequalities  $\| \mathbf{H}^S_t - \mathbf{B}^S_{j_m}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$  and  $\| \mathbf{w}^{I,S}_{t + 1} - \mathbf{w}^{S}_{t + 1}\| \leq M_1^S (\frac{r}{n} +\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}})$  hold for all iterations  $t$ .

Theorem 12 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones (all iterations)). Suppose that there are  $T$  iterations in total for each training phase, then with probability higher than  $1 - T \times (\Psi_{1} + \Psi_{2} + \Psi_{3})$ , for any  $t$  where  $j_{m} < t < j_{m} + T_{0} - 1$ ,  $\| \pmb{w}^{I,S}_{t} - \pmb{w}^{S}_{t}\| \leq \frac{1}{\frac{1}{2} - \frac{r}{n} - \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}} M_{1}\left(\frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}\right)$  and  $\|\pmb{H}^{S}_{t} - \pmb{B}^{S}_{j_{m}}\| \leq \xi_{j_{1},j_{m}}^{S}$ , where  $\xi_{j_{1},j_{m}}^{S}$  is defined in Corollary 2,  $\Psi_{1}$  is defined in Lemma 9,  $\Psi_{2}$  is defined in Theorem 8 and  $\Psi_{3}$  is defined in Lemma 10.

Then we have the following bound for  $\| \mathbf{w}^U t - \mathbf{w}^I t\|$

Theorem 13 (Main result: Bound between true and incrementally updated iterates for SGD). Suppose that there are  $T$  iterations in total for each training phase, then with probability higher than  $1 - T \times (\Psi_{1} + \Psi_{2} + \Psi_{3})$ , the result  $\boldsymbol{w}^{I,S_t}$  of Algorithm 1 approximates the correct iteration values  $\boldsymbol{w}^{U,S_t}$  at the rate

$$
\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} \| \leq o \left(\left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)\right).
$$

So  $\| \pmb{w}^{U,S}_t - \pmb{w}^{I,S}_t\|$  is of a lower order than  $(\frac{r}{n} +\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}})$ .

# A.3.4. PROOF OF THEOREM 10

Proof. By subtracting  $\mathbf{w}^{S}_{t} - \mathbf{w}^{U,S}_{t}$ , taking the matrix norm and using the update rule in equation (S5) and (S6), we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t + 1} \| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \eta G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t}\right) - \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \eta G _ {B - \Delta B, S} ^ {U} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right)\right) \right\| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \eta \left(G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t}\right) - G _ {B - \Delta B, S} ^ {U} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right)\right) \right\| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \eta \left(G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t}\right) - G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right) \right. \right. \tag {S54} \\ + G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} t\right) - G _ {B - \Delta B, S} ^ {U} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} t\right) \| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \eta \left(G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t}\right) - G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right)\right) + \right. \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\eta \left(G _ {B, S} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right) - G _ {B - \Delta B, S} ^ {U} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right)\right) \|
$$

By Cauchy mean-value theorem and the triangle inequality, the above formula becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \eta \left(\frac {1}{B} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t}\right)\right) d x\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right) \| + \eta \| \delta_ {t, S} \| \\ = \| \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta (\frac {1}{B} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {t}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t}\right)\right) d x)\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t}\right) \| + \eta \| \delta_ {t, S} \| \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the Lemma 3 and Lemma 9, the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\leq (1 - \eta \mu) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \| + \eta 2 c _ {2} \frac {\Delta B _ {t}}{B}
$$

Then by using Lemma 9 and using the formula above recursively, we get that with probability higher than  $1 - t \cdot \Psi_{1}$ ,  $\| \mathbf{w}^{S}_{t'} - \mathbf{w}^{U,S}_{t'} \| \leq \frac{2c_2}{\mu} \left( \frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}} \right)$  holds for all iterations  $t' \leq t$ , which finishes the proof.

![](images/6373af9856f7c05d01b97771091625fc74da992c92001f40e085505bacd780d3.jpg)

# A.3.5. PROOF OF THEOREM 11

Proof. For any  $t' \leq t$ , by subtracting  $\mathbf{w}^{S}_{t'}$  by  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S_{t'}}$  and taking the same argument as equation (S29)-(S31) (except that  $\mathbf{w}_{t'}$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t'}$ ,  $\mathbf{H}$ ,  $\mathbf{B}$ ,  $n, r$  are replaced with  $\mathbf{w}^{S}_{t'}$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S_{t'}}$ ,  $\mathbf{H}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{B}^S$ ,  $B$ ,  $\Delta B_{t'}$ ), the following equality holds due to the bound on  $\|\mathbf{H}^{S}_{t'} - \mathbf{B}^{S}_{j_m}\|$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} \right\| \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \eta \frac {B}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} \| + \frac {2 \Delta B _ {t} \eta c _ {2}}{B - \Delta B _ {t}}. \tag {S55} \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\frac{\Delta B_t}{B} \leq \frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}$  for all iterations between 0 and  $t$ , the following two inequalities hold:

$$
\frac {2 \Delta B _ {t} \eta c _ {2}}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} = \frac {2 \eta c _ {2}}{\frac {B}{\Delta B _ {t}} - 1} \leq \frac {2 \eta c _ {2}}{\frac {1}{\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} - 1} = \frac {2 \eta c _ {2}}{1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right), \tag {S56}
$$

$$
\frac {B}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} = \frac {1}{1 - \frac {\Delta B _ {t}}{B}} \leq \frac {1}{1 - \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)}. \tag {S57}
$$

Moreover, since Formula (S53) holds and  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0}^S +A\times M_1\left(\frac{(\log(p + 1))^2}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , then:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} = \xi_ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {x T _ {0}} A d _ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + m T _ {0} - 1} \\ + A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \\ \leq A d _ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + m T _ {0} - 1} + A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \\ = \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + m T _ {0} - 1} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \leq \frac {\mu}{2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then the Formula (S55) can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} \right\| \\ \leq (1 - \eta \mu + \eta \frac {B}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} (\xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} + A \times M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} \| + \frac {2 \Delta B _ {t} \eta c _ {2}}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} \| \\ \leq (1 - \eta \mu + \eta \frac {\xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} + A \times M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}}{1 - (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}})} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime}} \| + \frac {2 \eta c _ {2}}{1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}), \\ \end{array}
$$

which uses equation (S56) and (S57). Then applying the formula recursively from iteration  $t$  to 0, we can get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t ^ {\prime} + 1} \right\| \\ \leq \frac {1}{\eta (\mu - \frac {\xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} + 2 c _ {2} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}}{1 - (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}})}}) \frac {2 \eta c _ {2}}{1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then since  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0}^S\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , the formula above can be further bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \frac {2 c _ {2}}{\left(1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \mu - \xi_ {j _ {0} , j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} - A \times M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \\ \leq \frac {2 c _ {2}}{(\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}) \mu} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/62062f31d0e8d01fef2f2e72758540133cf2e1af505aaeae1c3c3a723fef2a86.jpg)

# A.3.6. PROOF OF THEOREM 12

The proof is the same as the proof of Theorem 6 except that  $\mathbf{w}$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^I$ ,  $n$ ,  $r$ ,  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}$ ,  $\mathbf{H}$ ,  $\mathbf{B}$  need to be replaced by  $\mathbf{w}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S}$ ,  $B$ ,  $r$ ,  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{H}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{B}^S$  and the main theorems that the proof depends on will be replaced by Theorem 11 and Corollary 2. But we need some careful explanations for the probability, which is shown as:

Proof. We define the following event at a given iteration  $k$ :

$$
\Omega_ {0} (k) = \left\{\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {k} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {k} \right\| \leq M _ {1} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right) \right\},
$$

$$
\Omega_ {1} (k) = \{\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {k} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {k} \| \leq M _ {1} ^ {S} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}) \},
$$

$$
\Omega_ {2} (k) = \left\{\left\| \mathbf {H} ^ {S} _ {k - 1} - \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} \right\} \left(j _ {m} \leq k - 1 \leq j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1\right),
$$

$$
