\Omega_ {3} (k) = \{\| \left(\frac {1}{B} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {B} _ {k - 1}} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {k - 1})\right) - \mathbf {H} (\mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {k - 1}) \| \leq L \left(\frac {\log^ {2} (2 p)}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Omega_ {4} (k) = \left\{\xi_ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} A d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} \right. \\ + A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \} \text {w h e r e} j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0} \leq k - 1 \leq j _ {0} + (x + m) T _ {0} - 1, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\Omega_ {5} (k) = \{\frac {\Delta B _ {k - 1}}{B} \leq \frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} \}.
$$

For all  $t$ , according to Corollary 2, the following equation holds:

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 1} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1.
$$

in which the co-occurrence of multiple events is denoted by  $\bigcap$  or " $\),". So this formula means that the probability that \(\Omega_2(k)$  is true for all  $k \leq t$  given that the events  $\Omega_1(k)$  and  $\Omega_3(k)$  are true at the same time for all  $k \leq t$  is 1.

Similarly, according to Theorem 11,  $\operatorname*{Pr}(\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t}\Omega_{1}(k)\Big|\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t}\Omega_{2}(k),\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t}\Omega_{4}(k),\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t}\Omega_{5}(k)) = 1.$  Then we know that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k)) \cdot \Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 1} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) \\ = \Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 1} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1, \\ \end{array}
$$

which can be multiplied by

$$
\Pr \bigl(\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t - 1}\Omega_{1}(k)\bigr|\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t - 1}\Omega_{2}(k),\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t - 1}\Omega_{4}(k),\bigcap_{k = 1}^{t - 1}\Omega_{5}(k)\bigr).
$$

The result is then multiplied by

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 1} \Omega_ {2} (k) | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 2} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 1} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1.
$$

Then the following equality holds:

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t - 2} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1
$$

which uses the fact that  $\bigcap_{k=1}^{t} \Omega_y(k) \bigcap_{k=1}^{t-1} \Omega_y(k) = \bigcap_{k=1}^{t} \Omega_y(k)$  ( $y = 1,2,3,4,5$ ). So by repeating this until the iteration  $j_0$ , then the following equality holds:

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) \right\rvert   \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1 \tag {S58}
$$

When  $t \leq j_0$ , we know that  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S_t} = \mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}$  and  $M_1^S \geq M_1$ , which means that if  $\Omega_0(k)$  holds, then  $\Omega_1(k)$  holds when  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S_t} = \mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}$ , and thus

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k) | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {0} (k)) = 1.
$$

Then according to Theorem 10, we know that:

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {0} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {5} (k)) = 1.
$$

By multiplying the above two formulas, we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k) | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {0} (k)) \cdot \Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {0} (k) | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {5} (k)) \\ = \operatorname * {P r} (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {0} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {5} (k)) = 1 \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that since the probability of two joint events is smaller than that of either of the events, the following inequality holds:

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {0} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {5} (k)) \leq \Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {5} (k)) \leq 1.
$$

So we know that:

$$
\Pr (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {1} (k) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {j _ {0}} \Omega_ {5} (k)) = 1.
$$

which can be multiplied by Formula (S58) and thus the following equality holds:

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) \right\rvert   \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1 \tag {S59}
$$

Then we can compute the probability of the negation of the joint event  $(\bigcap_{k=1}^{t+1} \Omega_1(k), \bigcap_{k=1}^t \Omega_2(k))$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname * {P r} (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k))}} \\ = \operatorname * {P r} (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k)}} \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) \cdot \operatorname * {P r} (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) \\ + \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k)}} \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) \cdot \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)}) \\ \leq \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k)}}) \Big | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) + \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)}}) \\ = \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

The last two steps use the fact that

$$
\Pr \left(\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k) , \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k)}} \right\rvert   \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 0
$$

and

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k) \middle | \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)\right) \leq 1.
$$

By further using the property of the probability of the union of multiply events, the formula above is bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname * {P r} (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) \leq \operatorname * {P r} (\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k) \bigcup \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k) \bigcup \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) \\ \leq \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k)}}) + \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k)}}) + \Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using Theorem 8, Formula (S53), Lemma 9 and taking the union between iteration 0 and  $t$ , we get:

$$
\Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)}}) \leq t \times \Psi_ {2},
$$

$$
\Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k)}}) \leq t \Psi_ {3},
$$

$$
\Pr (\overline {{\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k)}}) \leq t \times \Psi_ {1}.
$$

Then we can know that:

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k)\right) \geq 1 - t \left(\Psi_ {2} + \Psi_ {3} + \Psi_ {1}\right)
$$

and thus

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k)\right) \geq \Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k)\right) \geq 1 - t \left(\Psi_ {2} + \Psi_ {3} + \Psi_ {1}\right).
$$

This finishes the proof.

Similarly, from Formula (S59), we know that for all  $T$  iterations:

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {2} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k) \right| \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {5} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {t} \Omega_ {3} (k)) = 1. \tag {S60}
$$

Through the same argument, we know that:

$$
\Pr \left(\bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {T} \Omega_ {1} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {T} \Omega_ {2} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {T} \Omega_ {4} (k), \bigcap_ {k = 1} ^ {T} \Omega_ {5} (k)\right) \geq 1 - T \left(\Psi_ {2} + \Psi_ {3} + \Psi_ {1}\right).
$$

# A.3.7. PROOF OF THEOREM 13

The proof is the same as the proof of Theorem 6 except that  $\mathbf{w}$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^I$ ,  $n$ ,  $r$ ,  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}$ ,  $\mathbf{H}$ ,  $\mathbf{B}$  need to be replaced by  $\mathbf{w}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S}$ ,  $B$ ,  $r$ ,  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{H}^S$ ,  $\mathbf{B}^S$  and the main theorems that the proof depends on will be replaced by Theorem 11 and Corollary 2. We will show some key steps below.

First of all, according to the proofs of Theorem 12, we know that the following inequalities hold with probability higher than  $1 - T(\Psi_{2} + \Psi_{3} + \Psi_{1})$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {k} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {k} \right\| \leq \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} M _ {1} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right); \\ \left\| \mathbf {H} ^ {S} _ {k} - \mathbf {B} ^ {S} _ {j _ {m}} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S}; \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \xi_ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} \leq (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} A d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} + A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4} \\ \leq \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}; \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\frac {\Delta B _ {k}}{B} \leq \frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}.
$$

Then by subtracting  $\mathbf{w}^{I,S_t}$  by  $\mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}$  and following the arguments from Formula (S32) to (S34), the following inequality holds for  $\| \mathbf{w}^{I,S_t} - \mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}\|$  with probability higher than  $1 - T\times (\Psi_{2} + \Psi_{1} + \Psi_{3})$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \right\| \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {B \eta}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S}\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \| \\ + \frac {\eta c _ {0}}{2} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} \| \| \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} \| + \frac {B \eta}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {S} _ {t} \| \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {B \eta}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} \eta}{2} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \| + \frac {B \eta}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} M _ {1} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

By using the fact that  $\frac{\Delta B_t}{B} \leq \frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{1/4}}$  and  $\xi_{j_1,j_m} \leq \xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m-1)T_0} + A \times M_1\left(\frac{(\log(p+1))^2}{B}\right)^{1/4}$ , the formula above can

be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \right\| \\ \leq \left(1 - \eta \mu + \frac {B \eta}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} \eta}{2} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}})\right) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \| + \frac {B \eta}{B - \Delta B _ {t}} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} ^ {S} M _ {1} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}) \\ \leq \left[ 1 - \eta \mu + \frac {\eta}{1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} (\xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} + A \times M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}) \right. \\ + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} \eta}{2} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}) ] \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} \| + \frac {\eta}{1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} \xi_ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}, j _ {0} + (x + m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} M _ {1} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\xi_{j_0,j_0 + (m - 1)T_0}^S +A\times M_1\left(\frac{(\log(p + 1))^2}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$  and  $B$  is a large mini-batch size, then

$$
1 - (\eta \mu - \frac {\eta}{1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}} (\xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} ^ {S} + A \times M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}) - \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} \eta}{2} (\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}})) <   1.
$$

Then after explicitly using the definition of  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}^S$  and following the argument of equation (S35) to (S38), we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {I} - \mathbf {w} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} ^ {U} \right\| \\ \leq (1 - \eta C) ^ {y T _ {0}} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + m T _ {0}} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {1} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)}{C \left(1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)} (1 - \eta C) ^ {y T _ {0}} (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {j _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} \frac {1}{1 - \left(\frac {1 - \eta \mu}{1 - \eta C}\right) ^ {T _ {0}}} \tag {S61} \\ + \frac {1}{1 - (1 - \eta C) ^ {T _ {0}}} \frac {M _ {1} \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)}{C \left(1 - \frac {r}{n} - \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)} \left(A _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + A _ {2} \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}} + A M _ {1} \left(\frac {(\log (p + 1)) ^ {2}}{B}\right) ^ {1 / 4}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

when  $t \to \infty$  and thus  $y \to \infty$ ,  $(1 - \eta C)^{yT_0} \to 0$ . Also with large mini-batch value  $B$ ,  $A_{1}\frac{r}{n} +A_{2}\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}} + AM_{1}\left(\frac{(\log(p + 1))^{2}}{B}\right)^{1 / 4}$  is a value of the same order as  $\frac{r}{n} +\frac{1}{B^{1 / 4}}$ . Thus

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {j _ {0} + (y + m) T _ {0}} \right\| = o \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right)
$$

and

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {U, S} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, S} _ {t} \right\| \leq o \left(\frac {r}{n} + \frac {1}{B ^ {1 / 4}}\right).
$$

# B. Details on applications

# B.1. Privacy related data deletion

The notion of Approximate Data Deletion from the training dataset is proposed in (Ginart et al., 2019):

Definition 1. A data deletion operation  $R_A$  is a  $\delta$ -deletion for algorithm  $A$  if, for all datasets  $D$  and for all measurable subset  $S$ , the following inequality holds:

$$
P r [ A (D _ {- i}) \in S | D _ {- i} ] \geq \delta P r [ R _ {A} (D, A (D), i) \in S | D _ {- i} ],
$$

where  $D$  is the full training dataset,  $D_{-i}$  is the remaining dataset after the  $i_{th}$  sample is removed,  $A(D)$  and  $A(D_{-i})$  represent the model trained over  $D$  and  $D_{-i}$  respectively. Also  $R_A$  is an approximate model update algorithm, which updates the model after the sample  $i$  is removed.

This definition mimics the classical definition of differential privacy (Dwork et al., 2014):

Definition 2. A mechanism  $M$  is  $\epsilon$ -differentially private, where  $\epsilon \geq 0$ , if for all neighboring databases  $D_0$  and  $D_1$ , i.e., for databases differing in only one record, and for all sets  $S \in [M]$ , where  $[M]$  is the range of  $M$ , the following inequality holds:

$$
P r [ M (D _ {0}) \in S ] \leq e ^ {\epsilon} P r [ M (D _ {1}) \in S ].
$$

By borrowing the notations from (Ginart et al., 2019), we define a version of approximate data deletion, which is slightly more strict than the one from (Ginart et al., 2019):

Definition 3.  $R_{A}$  is an  $\epsilon$ -approximate deletion for  $A$  if for all  $D$  and measurable subset  $S \subset \mathcal{H}$ :

$$
P (A (D _ {- i}) \in S | D _ {- i}) \leq e ^ {\epsilon} P (R _ {A} (D, A (D), i) \in S | D _ {- i})
$$

and

$$
P \left(R _ {A} (D, A (D), i) \in S | D _ {- i}\right) \leq e ^ {\epsilon} P \left(A (D _ {- i}) \in S | D _ {- i}\right).
$$

To satisfy this definition for gradient descent, necessary randomness is added to the output of the BaseL and DeltaGrad. One simple way is the Laplace mechanism (Dwork et al., 2014), also following the idea from (Chaudhuri & Monteleoni, 2009) where noise following the Laplace distribution, i.e.

$$
L a p (x | \frac {2}{n \epsilon \lambda}) = \frac {1}{\frac {2}{n \epsilon \lambda}} \exp (- \frac {| x |}{\frac {2}{n \epsilon \lambda}}),
$$

is added to the each coordinate of the output of the regularized logistic regression. Here  $p$  is the number of the parameters,  $\lambda$  is the regularization rate and  $\frac{2}{n\lambda}$  is the sensitivity of logistic regression (see (Chaudhuri & Monteleoni, 2009) for more details).

We can add even smaller noise to  $\mathbf{w}^*$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{U^*}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^{I^*}$ , which follows the distribution  $Lap\left(\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}\right)$  for each coordinate of  $\mathbf{w}^*$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{U^*}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^{I^*}$  and is independent across different coordinates. Here  $\delta > \sqrt{p}\delta_0$  and

$$
\delta_ {0} = \frac {1}{\eta (\frac {1}{2} \mu - \frac {r}{n - r} \mu - \frac {c _ {0} M _ {1} r}{2 n}) ^ {2}} \frac {M _ {1} r}{n - r} (A \frac {1}{\frac {1}{2} - \frac {r}{n}} M _ {1} \frac {r}{n})
$$

(which is an upper bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^{U^*} - \mathbf{w}^{I^*}\|$ ), such that the randomized DeltaGrad preserves  $\epsilon$ -approximate deletion.

Proof. We denote the model parameters after adding the random noise over  $\mathbf{w}^R$ ,  $\mathbf{w}^{U,R}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^{I,R}$ , and  $\mathbf{v}_i$  as the value of  $\mathbf{v}$  in the  $i_{th}$  coordinate. We have:

$$
\mathbf {w} ^ {*} - \mathbf {w} ^ {R ^ {*}}, \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U, R ^ {*}}, \mathbf {w} ^ {I ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I, R ^ {*}} \sim L a p (\frac {\delta}{\epsilon})
$$

Given an arbitrary vector  $\mathbf{z} = [z_1, z_2, \ldots, z_p]$ , the probability density ratio between  $Pdf(\mathbf{w}^{U,R^*} = \mathbf{z})$  and  $Pdf(\mathbf{w}^{I,R^*} = \mathbf{z})$  can be calculated as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {P d f (\mathbf {w} ^ {U , R ^ {*}} = \mathbf {z})}{P d f (\mathbf {w} ^ {I , R ^ {*}} = \mathbf {z})} = \frac {\Pi_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \frac {\epsilon}{\delta} \exp (- \frac {\epsilon | \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} |}{\delta})}{\Pi_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \frac {\epsilon}{\delta} \exp (- \epsilon \frac {| \mathbf {z} _ {i} - \mathbf {w} _ {i} ^ {I ^ {*}} |}{\delta})} \\ = \Pi_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \exp \left(\frac {\epsilon \left(\left| \mathbf {z} _ {i} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} \right| - \left| \mathbf {z} _ {i} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I _ {i} ^ {*}} \right|\right)}{\delta}\right) \\ \leq \Pi_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \exp (\frac {\epsilon (| \mathbf {w} _ {i} ^ {*} - \mathbf {w} _ {i} ^ {U} |)}{\delta}) \\ = \exp \left(\frac {\epsilon \left(\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} \| _ {1}\right)}{\delta}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Since

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} \right\| _ {1} \leq \sqrt {p} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} \right\| _ {2} = \sqrt {p} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} \right\|
$$

Then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {P d f \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U , R ^ {*}} = \mathbf {z}\right)}{P d f \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I , R ^ {*}} = \mathbf {z}\right)} \leq \exp \left(\frac {\epsilon \left(\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I ^ {*}} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U ^ {*}} \|\right)}{\delta}\right) \\ \leq \exp (\frac {\epsilon \sqrt {p} \delta_ {0}}{\delta}) \leq \exp (\epsilon) \\ \end{array}
$$

Similarly, we can also prove  $\frac{Pdf(\mathbf{w}^{U,R^*} = \mathbf{z})}{Pdf(\mathbf{w}^{I,R^*} = \mathbf{z})} \geq \exp(\epsilon)$  by symmetry.

![](images/df514f71765cb3410ba1ddcaec3f3c725dc2cd92c97ddb7b08362871c5c5da47.jpg)

# C. Supplementary algorithm details

In Section, we only provided the details of DeltaGrad for deterministic gradient descent for the strongly convex and smooth objective functions in batch deletion/addition scenarios. In this section, we will provide more details on how to extend DeltaGrad to handle stochastic gradient descent, online deletion/addition scenarios and non-strongly convex, non-smooth objective functions.

# C.1. Extension of DeltaGrad for stochastic gradient descent

By using the notations from equations (S5)-(S7), we need to approximately or explicitly compute  $G_{B,S}$ , i.e. the average gradient for a mini-batch in the SGD version of DeltaGrad, instead of  $\nabla F$ , which is the average gradient for all samples. So by replacing  $\mathbf{w}_t, \mathbf{w}^U_t, \mathbf{w}^I_t, \nabla F, \mathbf{B}$  and  $\mathbf{H}$  with  $\mathbf{w}^S_t, \mathbf{w}^{U,S_t}, \mathbf{w}^{I,S_t}, G_{B,S}, \mathbf{B}^S$  and  $\mathbf{H}^S$  in Algorithm 1, we get the SGD version of DeltaGrad.

# C.2. Extension of DeltaGrad for online deletion/addition

In the online deletion/addition scenario, whenever the model parameters are updated after the deletion or addition of one sample, the history information should be also updated to reflect the changes. By assuming that only one sample is deleted or added each time, the online deletion/addition version of DeltaGrad is provided in Algorithm 3 and the differences relative to Algorithm 1 are highlighted.

Since the history information needs to be updated every time when new deletion or addition requests arrive, we need to do some more analysis on the error bound, which is still pretty close to the analysis in Section A.

In what follows, the analysis will be conducted on gradient descent with online deletion. Other similar scenarios, e.g. stochastic gradient descent with online addition, will be left as the future work.

# C.2.1. CONVERGENCE RATE ANALYSIS FOR ONLINE GRADIENT DESCENT VERSION OF DELTAGRAD

# Additional notes on setup, preliminaries

Let us still denote the model parameters for the original dataset at the  $t_{th}$  iteration by  $\mathbf{w}_t$ . During the model update phase for the  $k_{th}$  deletion request at the  $t_{th}$  iteration, the model parameters updated by BaseL and DeltaGrad are denoted by  $\mathbf{w}_t^U(k)$  and  $\mathbf{w}_t^I(k)$  respectively where  $\mathbf{w}_t^U(0) = \mathbf{w}_t^I(0) = \mathbf{w}_t$ . We also assume that the total number of removed samples in all deletion requests,  $r$ , is still far smaller than the total number of samples,  $n$ .

Also suppose that the indices of the removed samples are  $\{i_1, i_2, \ldots, i_r\}$ , which are removed at the  $1_{st}, 2_{nd}, 3_{rd}, \ldots, r_{th}$  deletion request. This also means that the cumulative number of samples up to the  $k_{th}$  deletion request ( $k \leq r$ ) is  $n - k$  for all  $1 \leq k \leq r$  and thus the objective function at the  $k_{th}$  iteration will be:

$$
F ^ {k} (\mathbf {w}) = \frac {1}{n - k} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {k}} F _ {i} (\mathbf {w}).
$$

where  $R_{k} = \{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots ,i_{k}\}$ . Plus, at the  $k_{th}$  deletion request, we denote by  $\mathbf{H}_t^k$  the average Hessian matrix of  $F^{k}(\mathbf{w})$

Algorithm 3 DeltaGrad (online deletion/addition)  
Input :The full training set  $(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})$  , model parameters cached during the training phase for the full training samples  $\{\mathbf{w}_0,\mathbf{w}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{w}_t\}$  and corresponding gradients  $\{\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_0),\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_1),\ldots ,\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)\}$  , the index of the removed training sample or the added training sample  $i_r$  period  $T_0$  total iteration number  $T$  , history size  $m$  , warmup iteration number  $j_{0}$  , learning rate  $\eta$  Output:Updated model parameter  $\mathbf{W}^{I_t}$    
25 Initialize  $\mathbf{w}^I_0\gets \mathbf{w}_0$    
26 Initialize an array  $\Delta G = []$    
27 Initialize an array  $\Delta W = []$    
28 for  $t = 0;t <   T;t + +$  do   
29 if  $[(t - j_0)\mod T_0) == 0]$  or  $t\leq j_0$  then   
30 compute  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t})$  exactly   
31 compute  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}) - \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t})$  based on the cached gradient  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)$    
32 set  $\Delta G[k] = \nabla F(w^I_t) - \nabla F(w_t)$    
33 set  $\Delta W[k] = w^{I}_{t} - w_{t}$  , based on the cached parameters  $\mathbf{W}_t$    
34  $k\gets k + 1$    
35 compute  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1}$  by using exact GD update (equation (1))   
36  ${\bf w}_t\gets {\bf w}^I_t$    
37  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)\gets \nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_t)$    
else   
39 Pass  $\Delta W[-m]:$ $\Delta G[-m]:$  , the last m elements in  $\Delta W$  and  $\Delta G$  , which are from the  $j_{1}^{th},j_{2}^{th},\ldots ,j_{m}^{th}$  iterations where  $j_{1} <   j_{2} <   \dots < j_{m}$  depend on  $t,\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{w}^{I}_{t} - \mathbf{w}_{t}$  , and the history size  $m$  , to the L-BFGFS Algorithm (See Supplement) to get the approximation of  $\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w}_t)\mathbf{v}$  i.e.,  $\mathbf{B}_{j_m}\mathbf{v}$    
40 Approximate  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t) = \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t) + \mathbf{B}_{j_m}(\mathbf{w}^I_t - \mathbf{w}_t)$    
41 Compute  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1}$  by using the "leave-1-out" gradient formula, based on the approximated  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t})$    
42  ${\bf w}_t\gets {\bf w}^I_t$    
43  $\nabla F({\bf w}_t)\gets \frac{\eta}{n - 1} [n({\bf B}_{j_m}({\bf w}^I_t - {\bf w}_t) + \nabla F({\bf w}_t)) - \nabla F_{i_r}({\bf w}_t)]$    
end   
45 end   
46 return  $\mathbf{w}^{I_t}$

evaluated between  $\mathbf{w}^I t(k + 1)$  and  $\mathbf{w}^I t(k)$ :

$$
\mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n - k} \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {k}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k) + x (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k + 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k))) d x
$$

Specifically,

$$
\mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {0} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (0) + x (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (0))) d x.
$$

Also the model parameters and the approximate gradients evaluated by DeltaGrad at the  $r - 1_{st}$  deletion request are used at the  $r_{th}$  request, and are denoted by:

$$
\left\{\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {0} (r - 1), \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {1} (r - 1), \dots , \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \right\}
$$

and

$$
\left\{g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {0} (r - 1)\right), g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {1} (r - 1)\right), \dots , g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\}.
$$

Note that  $g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(k))(k\leq r)$  is not necessarily equal to  $\nabla F$  due to the approximation brought by DeltaGrad. But due to the periodicity of DeltaGrad, at iteration 0, 1, ...,  $j_{0}$  and iteration  $j_0 + xT_0$  ( $x = 1,2,\dots$ ), the gradients are explicitly evaluated, i.e.:

$$
g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (k)) = \frac {1}{n - k} \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {k}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (k))
$$

for  $t = 0,1,\ldots ,j_0$  or  $t = j_0 + xT_0$ $(x\geq 1)$  and all  $k\leq r$

Also, due to the periodicity, the sequence  $\left[\Delta g_{j_0},\Delta g_{j_1},\dots ,\Delta g_{j_{m - 1}}\right]$  used in approximating the Hessian matrix always uses the exact gradient information, which means that:

$$
\Delta g _ {j _ {q}} = \frac {1}{n - k} \left[ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {k}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {q}} (k)\right) - \sum_ {i \notin R _ {k}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {q}} (k - 1)\right) \right]
$$

where  $q = 1,2,\ldots ,m - 1$ . So Lemma 6 on the bound on the eigenvalues of  $B_{j_q}$  holds for all  $q$  and  $k = 1,2,\dots,r$ .

But for the iterations where the gradients are not explicitly evaluated, the calculation of  $g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(k))$  depends on the approximated Hessian matrix  $\mathbf{B}_{j_m}^{k-1}$  and the approximated gradients calculated at the  $t_{th}$  iteration at the  $k-1$ -st deletion request. So the update rule for  $g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(k))$  is:

$$
\begin{array}{l} g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (k)\right) = \frac {1}{n - k} \left\{\left(n - k + 1\right) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {k - 1} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (k) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (k - 1)\right) \right. \right. \tag {S62} \\ \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k - 1)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {k}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k)\right) \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here the product  $\mathbf{B}_{j_m}^{k - 1}\cdot (\mathbf{w}^I_t(k) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(k - 1))$  approximates

$$
\frac {1}{n - k + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {k - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k)\right) - \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k - 1)\right)
$$

and  $g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(k - 1))$  approximates  $\frac{1}{n - k + 1}\sum_{i\notin R_{k - 1}}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t(k - 1)).$

Similarly, the online version of  $\Delta w$  (at the  $k_{th}$  iteration) becomes:

$$
\Delta w _ {j _ {q}} (k) = \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {q}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {q}} (k - 1)
$$

where  $q = 1,2,\ldots ,m - 1$

Similarly, we use  $d_{j_a,j_b}(k)$  to denote the value of the upper bound  $d$  on the distance between the iterates at the  $k_{th}$  deletion request and use  $\mathbf{B}_{jm}^{k - 1}$  to denote the approximated Hessian matrix in the  $k_{th}$  deletion request, which approximated the Hessian matrix  $\mathbf{H}_t^{k - 1}$ .

So the update rule for  $\mathbf{w}^I_t(k)$  becomes:

$$
\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (k) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k) - \frac {\eta}{n - k} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {k}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k)\right), [ (t - j _ {0}) \mod T _ {0} = 0 ] o r t \leq j _ {0} \\ \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k) - \frac {\eta}{n - k} \{(n - k + 1) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {k - 1} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k - 1)\right) \right. \\ \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k - 1)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {k}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k)\right) \}, e l s e. \end{array} \right. \tag {S63}
$$

# Proof preliminaries.

On each deletion request, the BaseL model parameters are retrained from scratch on the remaining samples. This implies that Theorem 4 still holds, if we replace  $\mathbf{w}^U_t, \mathbf{w}_t$  and  $r$  with  $\mathbf{w}^U_t(k), \mathbf{w}^U_t(k - 1)$  and 1 respectively:

Theorem 14 (Bound between iterates deleting one datapoint).  $\| \pmb{w}^U_t(r) - \pmb{w}^U_t(r - 1)\| \leq M_1\frac{1}{n}$  where  $M_{1} = \frac{2}{\mu} c_{2}$  is some positive constant that does not depend on  $t$ . Here  $\mu$  is the strong convexity constant, and  $c_{2}$  is the bound on the individual gradients.

By induction, we have:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \right\| = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (0) \right\| \leq M _ {1} \frac {r}{n}. \tag {S64}
$$

Then let us do some analysis on  $d_{j_a,j_b}(k)$ . We use the notation  $M_1^r\frac{1}{n}$  for  $\frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{r + 1}{n} - \frac{2(r - 1)}{n}\left(\frac{2L + \mu}{\mu}\right)}$ , where  $M_1^r$  is a constant which does not depend on  $k$ .

Lemma 11. If  $\| \pmb{w}^I_t(k) - \pmb{w}^I_t(k - 1)\| \leq \frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{k + 1}{n} - \frac{2(k - 1)}{n}\left(\frac{2L + \mu}{\mu}\right)}$  for all  $k \leq r$ , then  $d_{j_a,j_b}(r) \leq d_{j_a,j_b}(0) + 2r \cdot M_1^r\frac{1}{n}$  where  $M_1$  is defined in Theorem 14.

Proof. Recall that  $d_{j_a,j_b}(k) = \max (\| \mathbf{w}^I_y(k) - \mathbf{w}^I_z(k)\|)_{j_a < y < z < j_b}$ . Then for two arbitrary iterations  $y, z$ , let us bound  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_y(k) - \mathbf{w}^I_z(k)\|$  as below:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k) \right\| \\ = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k) + \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k - 1) + \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k - 1) \right\| \\ \leq \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k - 1) \| + \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k - 1) \| + \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {z} (k - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {y} (k - 1) \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t(k) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(k - 1)\|$ , the above formula leads to:

$$
\leq 2 \cdot \frac {\frac {2 M _ {1}}{n}}{1 - \frac {k + 1}{n} - \frac {2 (k - 1)}{n} (\frac {2 L + \mu}{\mu})} + \| \mathbf {w} _ {z} ^ {I} (k - 1) - \mathbf {w} _ {y} ^ {I} (k - 1) \|.
$$

By using that  $\frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{k + 1}{n} - \frac{2(k - 1)}{n}\left(\frac{2L + \mu}{\mu}\right)} \leq \frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{r + 1}{n} - \frac{2(r - 1)}{n}\left(\frac{2L + \mu}{\mu}\right)}$  and applying it recursively for  $k = 1, 2, \ldots, r$ , we have:

$$
\| \mathbf {w} _ {y} ^ {I} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {z} ^ {I} (r) \| \leq 2 r \cdot \frac {\frac {2 M _ {1}}{n}}{1 - \frac {r + 1}{n} - \frac {2 (r - 1)}{n} (\frac {2 L + \mu}{\mu})} + \| \mathbf {w} _ {z} ^ {I} (0) - \mathbf {w} _ {y} ^ {I} (0) \|.
$$

Then by using the definition of  $d_{j_a,j_b}(k)$ , the following inequality holds:

$$
d _ {j _ {a}, j _ {b}} (r) \leq d _ {j _ {a}, j _ {b} + T _ {0} - 1} (0) + 2 r \cdot \frac {\frac {2 M _ {1}}{n}}{1 - \frac {r + 1}{n} - \frac {2 (r - 1)}{n} \left(\frac {2 L + \mu}{\mu}\right)}.
$$

Recalling the definition of  $M_1^r \frac{1}{n}$ , this is exactly the required result.

We also mention that, since  $\frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{k + 1}{n} - \frac{2(k - 1)}{n}\left(\frac{2L + \mu}{\mu}\right)} \leq \frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{r + 1}{n} - \frac{2(r - 1)}{n}\left(\frac{2L + \mu}{\mu}\right)}$ , then  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t(k) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(k - 1)\| \leq M_1^r\frac{1}{n}$  for any  $k \leq r$ .

Theorem 15. Suppose that at the  $k_{th}$  deletion request,  $\| \pmb{w}^{I}_{j_q}(k) - \pmb{w}^{I}_{j_q}(k - 1)\| \leq M_1^r\frac{1}{n}$ , where  $q = 1,2,\ldots,m$  and  $M_1 = \frac{2c_2}{\mu}$ . Let  $e = \frac{L(L + 1) + K_2L}{\mu K_1}$  for the upper and lower bounds  $K_{1},K_{2}$  on the eigenvalues of the quasi-Hessian from Lemma 6, and for the Lipschitz constant  $c_{0}$  of the Hessian. For  $1\leq z + 1\leq y\leq m$  we have:

$$
\| \boldsymbol {H} _ {j _ {z}} ^ {k - 1} - \boldsymbol {H} _ {j _ {y}} ^ {k - 1} \| \leq c _ {0} d _ {j _ {z}, j _ {y}} (k - 1) + c _ {0} M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \Delta g _ {j _ {z}} - \boldsymbol {B} _ {j _ {y}} ^ {k - 1} \Delta w _ {j _ {z}} \right\| \leq \left[ (1 + e) ^ {y - z - 1} - 1 \right] \cdot c _ {0} \left(d _ {j _ {z}, j _ {y}} + M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}\right) \cdot s _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (k - 1)
$$

where  $s_{j_1,j_m}(k - 1) = \max \left(\| \Delta w_a(k - 1)\|\right)_{a = j_1,j_2,\ldots ,j_m} = \max \left(\| \pmb{w}^{I,S_a}(k - 1) - \pmb{w}^{I,S_a}(k - 2)\|\right)_{a = j_1,j_2,\ldots ,j_m}$ . Recall that  $d$  is defined as the maximum gap between the steps of the algorithm for the iterations from  $j_z$  to  $j_y$ :

$$
d _ {j _ {z}, j _ {y}} (k - 1) = \max  \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {a} (k - 1) - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {b} (k - 1) \|\right) _ {j _ {z} \leq a \leq b \leq j _ {y}}. \tag {S65}
$$

Proof. Let us bound the difference between the averaged Hessians  $\| \mathbf{H}_{j_z}^{k - 1} - \mathbf{H}_{j_y}^{k - 1}\|$ , where  $1\leq z < y\leq m$ , using their

definition, as well as using Assumption 4 on the Lipschitzness of the Hessian. First we can get the following equality:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {j _ {y}} ^ {k - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {j _ {z}} ^ {k - 1} \right\| \\ = \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left[ \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1)\right)\right) \right] d x \\ - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left[ \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1)\right)\right) \right] d x \| \tag {S66} \\ = \| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left[ \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1)\right)\right) \right. \\ \left. - \mathbf {H} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1)\right)\right) \right] d x \| \\ \end{array}
$$

Then we can bound this as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq c _ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1)\right) \\ - \left[ \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1)\right) \right] \| d x \\ \leq c _ {0} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1) \| \\ + \frac {c _ {0}}{2} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) - \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1)\right) \| \\ \leq c _ {0} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1) \| \\ + \frac {c _ {0}}{2} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {z}} (k - 1) \| + \frac {c _ {0}}{2} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {j _ {y}} (k - 1) \| \\ \leq c _ {0} d _ {j _ {y}, j _ {z}} (k - 1) + c _ {0} M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \leq c _ {0} d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (k - 1) + c _ {0} M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

On the last line, we have used the definition of  $d_{j_z,j_y}$ , and the assumption on the boundedness of  $\| \mathbf{w}^{I}_{j_z}(k) - \mathbf{w}^{I}_{j_z}(k - 1)\|$ . Then by following the rest of the proof of Theorem 3, we get:

$$
\| \Delta g _ {j _ {z}} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {y}} \Delta w _ {j _ {z}} \| \leq \big [ (1 + e) ^ {y - z - 1} - 1 \big ] \cdot c _ {0} (d _ {j _ {z}, j _ {y}} (k - 1) + M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}) \cdot s _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (k - 1).
$$

Similarly, the online version of Corollary 1 also holds by following the same derivation as the proof of Corollary 1 (except that  $r, \xi_{j_1,j_m}$  and  $d_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1}$  is replaced by  $1, \xi_{j_1,j_m}(k - 1)$  and  $d_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1}(k - 1)$  respectively), i.e.:

Corollary 3 (Approximation accuracy of quasi-Hessian to mean Hessian (online deletion)). Suppose that at the  $k_{th}$  deletion request,  $\| \pmb{w}^{I}_{j_s}(k) - \pmb{w}^{I}_{j_s}(k - 1)\| \leq M_1^r\frac{1}{n}$  and  $\| \pmb{w}^{I}_t(k) - \pmb{w}^{I}_t(k - 1)\| \leq M_1^r\frac{1}{n}$  where  $s = 1,2,\ldots ,m$ . Then for  $j_m\leq t\leq j_m + T_0 - 1$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {H} _ {t} ^ {k - 1} - \boldsymbol {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {k - 1} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (k - 1) := A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (k - 1) + A M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}. \tag {S67}
$$

Recall that  $A = \frac{c_0\sqrt{m}[(1 + e)^m - 1]}{c_1} + c_0$ , where  $c_0$  is the Lipschitz constant of the Hessian,  $c_1$  is the "strong independence" constant from Assumption 5, and  $d_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1}(k - 1)$  is the maximal gap between the iterates of the GD algorithm on the full data from  $j_1$  to  $j_m + T_0 - 1$  after the  $k - 1$ -st deletion.

Based on this, let us derive a bound on  $\| \nabla F_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r))\|$ ,  $\| g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r)) - \frac{1}{n - r}\sum_{i\notin R_{r}}\nabla F_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r))\|$  and  $\| g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r))\|$ .

Lemma 12. Suppose we are at an iteration  $t$  such that  $j_{m} \leq t \leq j_{m} + T_{0} - 1$ . If the following inequality holds for all  $k < r$ :

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k) - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k - 1) \right\| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n},
$$

then the following inequality holds for all  $i = 1,2,\dots ,n$ :

$$
\| \nabla F _ {i} (\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) \| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L r + c _ {2}.
$$

Proof. By adding and subtracting  $\nabla F_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r - 2))$  inside  $\| \nabla F_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r - 1))\|$ , we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\| \\ = \| \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) + \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \| \\ \leq \left\| \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right\| + \left\| \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right\| \\ \end{array}
$$

The last inequality uses the triangle inequality. Then by using the Cauchy mean value theorem, the upper bound on the eigenvalue of the Hessian matrix (i.e. Assumption 2) and the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t(k) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(k - 1)\|$ , the formula above is bounded as (recall  $\mathbf{H}_i$  is an integrated Hessian):

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \| \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) + x (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1))) \cdot (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)) \| + \| \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)) \| \\ \leq L M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + \| \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

By using this recursively, we get:

$$
\leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {r - 1} M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L + \| \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (0)) \| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L r + c _ {2}.
$$

Lemma 13. If at a given iteration  $t$  such that  $j_{m} \leq t \leq j_{m} + T_{0} - 1$ , for all  $k < r$ , the following inequalities hold:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k) - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (k - 1) \right\| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

and

$$
\xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (k - 1) \leq \frac {\mu}{2},
$$

then we have

$$
\| \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - g ^ {a} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \| \leq r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \mu .
$$

Proof. First of all,  $\frac{1}{n - r + 1}\sum_{i\notin R_{r - 1}}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1))$  can be rewritten as below by using the Cauchy mean-value theorem:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) = \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \left[ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r - 1}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \left\{\left(n - r + 2\right) \left[ \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 2} \times \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 2) \right] \right. \right. \\ + \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r - 1}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By subtracting the above formula from equation (S62), i.e., the update rule for the approximate gradient, the norm of the approximation error between true and approximate gradients is:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \| \\ = \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \| (n - r + 2) \left(\mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 2} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 2}\right) \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \\ + \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) - (n - r + 2) g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the triangle inequality, Corollary 3 on the approximation accuracy of the quasi-Hessian (where the bound is in terms of  $\xi$ ), and the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 2)\|$ , the formula above is bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \| \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 2} - \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 2} \| \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2) \| \\ + \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \| \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) - (n - r + 2) g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \| \tag {S68} \\ \leq \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 2) M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \| \frac {1}{n - r + 2} \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 2)) - g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 2)) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

By using that  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}(r - 2)\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , the formula above is bounded as:

$$
\leq \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \frac {\mu}{2} (M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}) + \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \| \frac {1}{n - r + 2} \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)) - g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)) \|
$$

We can use this recursively. Note that  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t(0)) = g^a (\mathbf{w}^I_t(0))$ . In the end, we get the following inequality:

$$
\leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {r - 1} \frac {n - k}{n - r} \frac {\mu}{2} \left(M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}\right) \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \frac {\mu}{2} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {r - 1} \frac {n - k}{n - r}
$$

Also for  $r \ll n$ ,  $\frac{n - k}{n - r} \leq 2$  (in fact we assumed  $r / n \leq \delta$  for a sufficiently small  $\delta$ , so this holds). So we get the bound  $rM_1^r \frac{1}{n} \mu$ .

![](images/c64963df7d21e384ffc93ed3f20713c67a991ea1f0547b53a0ee53eed493ed41.jpg)

Note that for  $\| \frac{1}{n - r + 1}\sum_{i\notin R_{r - 1}}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1)) - g^a (\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1))\|$ , we get a tighter bound when  $t\to \infty$  by using equation (S68), Lemma 11 (i.e.  $d_{j_a,j_b}(r)\leq d_{j_a,j_b}(0) + 2r\cdot M_1^r\frac{1}{n})$  and Lemma 8 without using  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}(r - 1)\leq \frac{\mu}{2}$ , which starts by bounding  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}(k - 1)$  where  $k <   = r$ ,  $j_{1} = j_{0} + xT_{0}$  and  $j_{m} = j_{0} + (x + m - 1)T_{0}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (k - 1) = A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (k - 1) + A M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \\ \leq A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (0) + 2 (k - 1) A \cdot M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + A M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \tag {S69} \\ \leq A (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} (0) + A (2 k - 1) M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which can be plugged into Equation (S68), i.e.:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \| \\ \leq \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 2) M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \\ + \frac {n - r + 2}{n - r + 1} \| \frac {1}{n - r + 2} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 2}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) - g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \| \\ \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {r - 1} \frac {n - k + 1}{n - k} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (k - 1) M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \tag {S70} \\ \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {r - 1} \frac {n - k + 1}{n - k} \left[ A (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} (0) + A (2 k - 1) M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \right] \cdot M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \\ \leq 2 A (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, m T _ {0} - 1} (0) r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + 2 A (r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}) ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

The last step uses that  $\frac{n - k + 1}{n - k} \leq 2$  and  $\sum_{k = 1}^{r - 1}(2k - 1) < \sum_{k = 1}^{r}(2k - 1) = r^2$ . So when  $t \to \infty$  and thus  $x \to \infty$ ,  $\| \frac{1}{n - r}\sum_{i \notin R_r}\nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r)) - g^a(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r))\| = o(\frac{r}{n})$ .

Then based on Lemma 12 and 13, the bound on  $\| g^{a}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}(r))\|$  becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\| \\ = \| g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) + \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \| \\ \leq \left\| g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\| + \left\| \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\| \tag {S71} \\ = r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \mu + M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L r + c _ {2} = (r \mu + L r) M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + c _ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

# Main results

Theorem 16 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones (online deletions)). Suppose that for any  $k < r$ ,  $\| \pmb{w}^I_t(k) - \pmb{w}^I_t(k - 1) \| \leq M_1^r \frac{1}{n}$ . At the  $r_{th}$  deletion request, consider an iteration  $t$  indexed with  $j_m$  for which  $j_m \leq t < j_m + T_0 - 1$ , and suppose that we are at the  $x$ -th iteration of full gradient updates, so  $j_1 = j_0 + xT_0$ ,  $j_m = j_0 + (m - 1 + x)T_0$ . Suppose that we have the bounds  $\| \pmb{H}_t^{r - 1} - \pmb{B}_{j_m}^{r - 1} \| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}(r - 1) = Ad_{j_1,j_m + T_0 - 1}(r - 1) + A(M_1^r \frac{1}{n})$  (where we recalled the definition of  $\xi$ ) and

$$
\xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 1) = A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (r - 1) + A \left(M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}\right) \leq \frac {\mu}{2}
$$

for all iterations  $x$ . Then

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) \right\| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}.
$$

Recall that  $c_{0}$  is the Lipschitz constant of the Hessian,  $M_{1}$  and  $A$  are defined in Theorem 14 and Corollary 3 respectively, and do not depend on  $t$ ,

Then by using the same derivation as the proof of Theorem 6, we get the following results at the  $r_{th}$  deletion request.

Theorem 17 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones (all iterations, online deletion)). At the deletion request  $r$ , if for all  $k < r$ ,  $\| \pmb{w}^I_t(k) - \pmb{w}^I_t(k - 1) \| \leq M_1^r \frac{1}{n}$  holds, then for any  $j_m < t < j_m + T_0 - 1$ ,

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r) - \boldsymbol {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1) \right\| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1} - \boldsymbol {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 1) := A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (r - 1) + A M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - g ^ {a} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\| \leq r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \mu
$$

hold

Then by induction (the base case is similar to Theorem 6), we know that the following theorem holds for all iterations  $t$ :

Theorem 18 (Bound between iterates on full data and incrementally updated ones (all iterations, all deletion requests, online deletion)). At the  $r_{th}$  deletion request, for any  $j_m < t < j_m + T_0 - 1$ ,

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r) - \boldsymbol {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1) \right\| \leq M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1} - \boldsymbol {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} \right\| \leq \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 1) := A d _ {j _ {1}, j _ {m} + T _ {0} - 1} (r - 1) + A M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - g ^ {a} \left(\boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\| \leq r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \mu
$$

hold

Then by induction (from the  $r_{th}$  deletion request to the  $1_{st}$  deletion request), the following inequality holds:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (0) \right\| = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \right\| \leq r \cdot M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}
$$

Then by using equation (S64), the following inequality holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \right\| = \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \right\| \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} \right\| + \left\| \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \right\| \tag {S72} \\ \leq M _ {1} \frac {r}{n} + (r - 1) \cdot M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} := M _ {2} \frac {r}{n} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $M_2$  is a constant which does not depend on  $t$  or  $k$ .

In the end, we get a similar result for the bound on  $\| \mathbf{w}^I_t(r) - \mathbf{w}^U_t(r)\|$ :

Theorem 19 (Convergence rate of DeltaGrad (online deletion)). At the  $r_{th}$  deletion request, for all iterations  $t$ , the result  $w^{I}_{t}(r)$  of DeltaGrad, Algorithm 3, approximates the correct iteration values  $w^{U}_{t}(r)$  at the rate

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) \right\| = o \left(\frac {r}{n}\right).
$$

So  $\| \pmb{w}^U_t(r) - \pmb{w}^I_t(r)\|$  is of a lower order than  $\frac{r}{n}$ .

# The proof of Theorem 16

Proof. Note that the approximated update rules for  $\mathbf{w}^I_t$  at the  $r_{th}$  and the  $(r - 1)_{st}$  deletion request are:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) = \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left\{\left(n - r + 1\right) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j m} ^ {r - 1} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right. \right. \tag {S73} \\ \left. \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) = \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \frac {\eta}{n - r + 1} \left\{\left(n - r + 2\right) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 2} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right. \right. \tag {S74} \\ \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r - 1}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that since

$$
\begin{array}{l} g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1)\right) = \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \left\{\left(n - r + 2\right) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 2} \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 2)\right) \right. \right. \\ \left. \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r - 1}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

then equation (S74) can be rewritten as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) = \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \frac {\eta}{n - r + 1} \left\{\left(n - r + 2\right) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 2} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right. \right. \\ \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 2)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r - 1}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \} \tag {S75} \\ = \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) - \eta g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by subtracting equation (S74) from equation (S75), the result becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) \\ = (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \{(n - r + 1) [ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) \\ \left. \left. + g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)) \right\} + \eta g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by adding and subtracting  $\mathbf{H}_t^{r - 1}$  and  $\frac{1}{n - r + 1}\sum_{i\notin R_{r - 1}}\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1))$  in the formula above and rearranging the result properly, it becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) \\ = \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n - r + 1}{n - r} \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right)\right) \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1)\right) \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left\{\left(n - r + 1\right) \left[ \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right. \right. \tag {S76} \\ + \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ \left. \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) \right\} + \eta g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the fact that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) + \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ = \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\left(\sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) = \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right),
$$

Equation (S76) becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} ^ {I} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} ^ {I} (r - 1) \\ = (\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n - r + 1}{n - r} \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right)) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left[ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) - \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right. \tag {S77} \\ \left. + (n - r + 1) g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] + \eta g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Also note that by using the Cauchy mean-value theorem, the following equation holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) - \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ = \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) - \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ = \left[ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right)\right) d x \right] \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

which can be plugged into equation (S77), i.e.:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) \\ = \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n - r + 1}{n - r} \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right)\right) \left(\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1)\right) \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left\{\left[ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right)\right) d x \right] \right. \tag {S78} \\ \cdot \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ + (n - r + 1) g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \} + \eta g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

which can be rearranged as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) \\ = \left(\mathbf {I} - \eta \frac {n - r + 1}{n - r} \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right)\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left\{\left[ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right)\right) d x \right] \right. \tag {S79} \\ \cdot \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by taking the matrix norm on both sides of equation (S79) and using that  $\| \mathbf{H}_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1) + x(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1)))\| \geq \mu$  and  $\| \mathbf{B}_{j_m}^{r - 1} - \mathbf{H}_t^{r - 1}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}(r - 1)$ , equation (S79) can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r - 1) \right\| \\ \leq (1 - \eta \mu) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \| \\ + \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 1) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \| \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \| \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) \| + \| \frac {\eta}{n - r} g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using Lemma 12 and equation (S71), the formula above becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq (1 - \eta \mu + \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r)) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) \| \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left(M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L (r - 1) + c _ {2}\right) + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left(M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} (r - 1) \mu + M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L (r - 1) + c _ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

By using the bound on  $\xi_{j_1,j_m}(r)$  and applying the above formula recursively across all iterations, the formula above becomes:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \frac {1}{\eta \mu - \frac {\eta (n - r + 1)}{n - r} \frac {\mu}{2}} (\frac {\eta}{n - r} (M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L (r - 1) + c _ {2}) \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left(M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} L (r - 1) + M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \mu (r - 1) + c _ {2}\right) \\ = \frac {2}{(n - r - 1) \mu} ((M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} (2 L (r - 1) + (r - 1) \mu) + 2 c _ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using that  $M_1 = \frac{2c_2}{\mu}$  and  $M_1^r \frac{1}{n} = \frac{\frac{2M_1}{n}}{1 - \frac{r + 1}{n} - \frac{2(r - 1)}{n} \left( \frac{2L + \mu}{\mu} \right)}$ , the formula above can be rewritten as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \frac {2}{(n - r - 1) \mu} \frac {\frac {2 M _ {1} (r - 1)}{n} (2 L + \mu) + \mu M _ {1} (1 - \frac {r + 1}{n} - \frac {2 (r - 1)}{n} (\frac {2 L + \mu}{\mu}))}{1 - \frac {r + 1}{n} - \frac {2 (r - 1)}{n} (\frac {2 L + \mu}{\mu})} \\ = \frac {2 \frac {M _ {1}}{n}}{1 - \frac {r + 1}{n} - \frac {2 (r - 1)}{n} (\frac {2 L + \mu}{\mu})} = M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

This finishes the proof.

# The proof of Theorem 19

Proof. Recall that the update rule for  $\mathbf{w}^U_t(r)$  is:

$$
\mathbf {w} _ {t + 1} ^ {U} (r) = \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U} (r) - \eta \frac {1}{n - r} \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {r}} \nabla F (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {U} (r))
$$

and the update rule for  $\mathbf{w}^I_t(r)$  is (where the gradients are explicitly evaluated):

$$
\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) = \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \frac {\eta}{n - r} [ (n - r + 1) (\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) + g ^ {a} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1))) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)) ].
$$

Then by subtracting  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1}(r)$  from  $\mathbf{w}^{U}_{t + 1}(r)$ , we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t + 1} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t + 1} (r) \| \\ = \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left\{\left(n - r + 1\right) \left[ \mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right. \right. \\ \left. \left. + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) \right\} + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r)\right) \| \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by bringing in  $\mathbf{H}_t^{r - 1}$  and  $\frac{1}{n - r + 1}\sum_{i\in R_{r - 1}}\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1))$  into the formula above, we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \| \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right) (\mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r) - \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {I} (r - 1)) \right. \\ + \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) + g ^ {a} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \\ - \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \in R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) + \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \in R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) ] \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} [ \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) + \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)) ] + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \not \in R _ {r}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r)) | |. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the triangle inequality and the result from equation (S70), the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right. \\ \left. + \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1} \times \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) + \frac {1}{n - r + 1} \sum_ {i \in R _ {r - 1}} \nabla F _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \left[ \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r)\right) - \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) + \nabla F _ {i _ {r}} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] \\ + \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \nabla F _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r)) \| + 2 A (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, (m - 1) T _ {0}} (0) r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + 2 A (r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}) ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that the first matrix norm in this formula is the same as equation (S33) by replacing  $n, r, \mathbf{w}^I_t, \mathbf{w}^U_t, \mathbf{w}_t, \mathbf{B}_{j_m}, \mathbf{H}_t$  and  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)$  with  $n - r + 1, 1, \mathbf{w}^I_t(r), \mathbf{w}^U_t(r), \mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1), \mathbf{B}_{j_m}^{r - 1}, \mathbf{H}_t^{r - 1}$  and  $\frac{1}{n - r + 1} \sum_{i \notin R_{r - 1}} \nabla F_i(\mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1))$  repr.. So by following the same derivation, the formula above can be bounded as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \| (\mathbf {I} - \frac {\eta}{n - r} \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \mathbf {H} _ {t, i} ^ {r - 1}) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r)\right) \| \\ + \| \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r)\right) \right] \| \\ + \| \frac {\eta}{n - r} [ \sum_ {i \notin R _ {r}} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) + x (\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1))) d x \\ - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1) + x \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right)\right) d x ] \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \| \\ + \| \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {B} _ {j _ {m}} ^ {r - 1} - \mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {r - 1}\right) \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r - 1)\right) \right] \| \\ + 2 A (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, (m - 1) T _ {0}} (0) r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} + 2 A (r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}) ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then by using the following facts:

1.  $\| \mathbf{I} - \eta \mathbf{H}_{t,i}^{r - 1}\| \leq 1 - \eta \mu$  
2. from Theorem 18 on the approximation accuracy of the quasi-Hessian to mean Hessian, we have the error bound  $\| \mathbf{H}_t^{r - 1} - \mathbf{B}_{j_m}^{r - 1}\| \leq \xi_{j_1,j_m}(r - 1)$  
3. we bound the difference of integrated Hessians using the strategy from Equation (S20);  
4. from Equation (S72), we have the error bound  $\| \mathbf{w}^U_t(r) - \mathbf{w}^I_t(r - 1)\| \leq M_2\frac{r}{n}$  (and this requires no additional assumptions),

the expression can be bounded as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq (1 - \eta \mu + \frac {(n - r + 1) \eta}{n - r} \xi_ {j _ {0}, j _ {0} + (m - 1) T _ {0}} (r - 1) + \frac {c _ {0} M _ {2} r \eta}{2 n}) \| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} \| \\ + \frac {M _ {2} (n - r + 1) r \eta}{n (n - r)} \xi_ {j _ {1}, j _ {m}} (r - 1) + 2 A (1 - \mu \eta) ^ {j _ {0} + x T _ {0}} d _ {0, (m - 1) T _ {0}} (0) r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n} \\ + 2 A \left(r M _ {1} ^ {r} \frac {1}{n}\right) ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which is very similar to equation (S36) (except the difference in the coefficient). So by following the derivation after equation (S36), we know that:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {w} ^ {I} _ {t} (r) - \mathbf {w} ^ {U} _ {t} (r) \right\| = o \left(\frac {r}{n}\right)
$$

when  $t\to \infty$

# C.3. Extension of DeltaGrad for non-strongly convex, non-smooth objective functions

For the original version of the L-BFGS algorithm, strong convexity is essential to make the secant condition hold. In this subsection, we present our extension of DeltaGrad to non-strongly convex, non-smooth objectives.

To deal with non-strongly convex objectives, we assume that convexity holds in some local regions. When constructing the arrays  $\Delta G$  and  $\Delta W$ , only the model parameters and their gradients where local convexity holds are used.

For local non-smoothness, we found that even a small distance between  $\mathbf{w}_t$  and  $\mathbf{w}^I_t$  can make the estimated gradient  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t)$  drift far away from  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)$ . To deal with this, we explicitly check if the norm of  $\mathbf{B}_{j_m}(\mathbf{w}_t - \mathbf{w}^I_t)$  (which equals to  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^I_t) - \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)$ ) is larger than the norm of  $L(\mathbf{w}_t - \mathbf{w}^I_t)$  for a constant  $L$ . In our experiments,  $L$  is configured as 1. The details of the modifications above are highlighted in Algorithm 4.

Algorithm 4 DeltaGrad (general models)  
Input :The full training set (X,Y), model parameters cached during the training phase for the full training samples  $\{\mathbf{w}_0,\mathbf{w}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{w}_t\}$  and corresponding gradients  $\{\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_0),\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_1),\ldots ,\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_t)\}$  , the removed training sample or the added training sample  $R$  period  $T_{0}$  total iteration number  $T$  history size  $m$  warmup iteration number  $j_0$  learning rate  $\eta$  Output:Updated model parameter  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}$    
47 Initialize  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_0\gets \mathbf{w}_0$    
48 Initialize an array  $\Delta G = []$    
49 Initialize an array  $\Delta W = []$    
50 Initialize last  $t = j_0$    
51 is_explicit  $=$  False   
52 for  $t = 0;t <   T;t + +$  do   
53 if  $(t - last_{t})$  mod  $T_0 = = 0$  or  $t\leq j_0$  then   
54 is_explicit  $=$  True   
55 else   
56 end   
57 if is_explicit  $= =$  True or  $t\leq j_0$  then   
58 last  $t = t$    
59 compute  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t})$  exactly   
60 compute  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}) - \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t})$  based on the cached gradient  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t})$  /\* check local convexity /\*/   
61 if  $<  \nabla F(\boldsymbol {w}_t^i) - \nabla F(\boldsymbol {w}_t),\boldsymbol {w}_t^i -\boldsymbol {w}_t > \leq 0$  then   
62 compute  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1}$  by using exact GD update (equation (1)) continue   
63   
64 end   
65 set  $\Delta G[k] = \nabla F(w^I_t) - \nabla F(w_t)$    
66 set  $\Delta W[k] = w^{I}_{t} - w_{t}$  , based on the cached parameters  $\mathbf{W}_t$    
67  $k\leftarrow k + 1$    
68 compute  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1}$  by using exact GD update (equation (1))   
69 else   
70 Pass  $\Delta W[-m]:,\Delta G[-m]:$  , the last m elements in  $\Delta W$  and  $\Delta G$  , which are from the  $j_{1}^{th},j_{2}^{th},\ldots ,j_{m}^{th}$  iterations where   
71  $j_{1} <   j_{2} <   \dots < j_{m}$  depend on  $t,\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{w}^{I}_{t} - \mathbf{w}_{t}$  , and the history size m, to the L-BFGFS Algorithm (See Supplement) to get   
72 the approximation of  $\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{w}_t)\mathbf{v}$  , i.e.,  $\mathbf{B}_{jm}\mathbf{v}$  /\* check local smoothness /\*/   
73 end   
74 Approximate  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}) = \nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t}) + \mathbf{B}_{jm}(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t} - \mathbf{w}_{t})$    
75 Compute  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t + 1}$  by using the "leave-r-out" gradient formula, based on the approximated  $\nabla F(\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t})$    
76 end   
77 end   
78 return  $\mathbf{w}^{I}_{t}$

# D. Supplementary experiments

In this section, we present some supplementary experiments that could not be presented in the paper due to space limitations.

# D.1. Experiments with large deletion rate

In this experiment, instead of deleting at most  $1\%$  of training samples each time as we did in Section 18 in the main paper, we vary the deletion rate from 0 to up to  $20\%$  on MNIST dataset and still compare the performance between DeltaGrad (with  $T_{0}$  as 5 and  $j_{0}$  as 10) and BaseL. All other hyper-parameters such as the learning rate and mini-batch size remain the same as in Section 18 in the main paper.

![](images/4ab938de2cd668eb8f2f6576e19a9410fe54ba53bba39598b55c52ea00df3a13.jpg)  
Figure S1. Running time and distance with varied deletion rate up to  $20\%$

The experimental results in Figure S1 show that even with the largest deletion rate, i.e.  $20\%$ , DeltaGrad can still be 1.67x faster than BaseL (2.27s VS 1.53s) and the error bound between their resulting model parameters (i.e.  $\mathbf{w}^{I*}$  VS  $\mathbf{w}^{U*}$ ) are still acceptable (on the order of  $10^{-3}$ ), far smaller than the error bound between  $\mathbf{w}^{U*}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^*$  (on the order of  $10^{-1}$ ). Such a small difference between  $\mathbf{w}^{I*}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^{U*}$  also results in almost the same prediction performance, i.e.  $87.460 \pm 0.0011\%$  and  $87.458 \pm 0.0012\%$  respectively. This experiment thus provides some justification for the feasibility of DeltaGrad even when the number of the removed samples is not far smaller than the entire training dataset size.

![](images/749462b2d7abc34577992b103f8b5aa902ddad222b1d90f4c2ff95787510ccfc.jpg)  
Figure S2. Running time and distance comparison with varying mini-batch size under fixed  $j_0 = 10$  and varying  $T_0$  ( $T_0 = 20$  VS  $T_0 = 10$  VS  $T_0 = 5$ )

![](images/920c488ba63c307be7a8849e5a6745d0fa1f98162fc992288ce5a7a9b0827cd4.jpg)

![](images/28e7bd657d785709683724cd8177d7fdd39210540faad60b602f2156f19406b3.jpg)

# D.2. Influence of hyper-parameters on performance

To begin with, the influence of different hyper-parameters used in SGD and DeltaGrad is explored. We delete one sample from the training set of MNIST by running regularized logistic regression with the same learning rate and regularization rate as in Section 18 and varying mini-batch sizes (1024 - 60000),  $T_0$  ( $T_0 = 20, 10, 5$ ) and  $j_0$  ( $j_0 = 5, 10, 50$ ). The experimental results are presented in Figure S2-S3. For different mini-batch sizes, we also used different epoch numbers to make sure that the total number of running iterations/steps in SGD are roughly the same. In what follows, we analyze how the mini-batch size, the hyper-parameters  $T_0$  and  $j_0$  influence the performance, thus providing some hints on how to choose proper hyper-parameters when DeltaGrad is used.

Influence of the mini-batch size. It is clear from Figure S2-S3 that with larger mini-batch sizes, DeltaGrad can gain more speed with longer running time for both BaseL and DeltaGrad. As discussed in Section 18, to compute the gradients, other GPU-related overhead (the overhead to copy data from CPU DRAM to GPU DRAM, the time to launch the kernel on

![](images/3eea752ad7fec2293828e93837c6b63e9767ec1e218f6172c31d4535b0bf61b7.jpg)  
Figure S3. Running time and distance comparison with varying mini-batch size under fixed  $T_0 = 5$  and varying  $j_0$  ( $j_0 = 5$  VS  $j_0 = 10$  VS  $j_0 = 50$ )

![](images/2f07775ae4117a03e1dd4c105424c4f1ac51fb9992af9d60800434c7cc95ab4a.jpg)

![](images/2ef9396dd5640f96a74787ba792ef27344e866bca9162cf469e9a60b08cf8975.jpg)

![](images/3674bf61dd042572f4f8945b7b169b5269ccbb733c287e4b17fc0ad3b4aac047.jpg)  
Figure S4. Comparison of DeltaGrad and PrIU

GPU) cannot be ignored. This can become more significant when compared against the smaller computational overhead for smaller mini-batch data. Also notice that, when  $T_0 = 5$ , with increasing  $B$ , the difference between  $\mathbf{w}^U$  and  $\mathbf{w}^I$  becomes smaller and smaller, which matches our conclusion in Theorem 13, i.e. with larger  $B$ , the difference  $o\left(\frac{r}{n} + \frac{1}{B^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)$  is smaller.

Influence of  $T_0$ . By comparing the three sub-figures in Figure S2, the running time slightly (rather than significantly) decreases with increasing  $T_0$  for the same mini-batch size.
